## management-governance — module synthesis

_Source: `99_management-governance-synthesis.md`_

# Management-Governance Module — HAIER (Synthesis)

## Abstract

Haier Smart Home's stewardship reads as standard and mixed — a stable, internally promoted CEO and an orderly CFO succession sit above a dividend-led capital-allocation record free of goodwill impairment, but whose newest invested capital turned value-destructive (incremental ROIC -8.3% in FY2025) even as the payout ratio keeps rising. Long-term pay is anchored entirely to ROE and profit-growth hurdles that genuinely missed in FY2025; individual insiders hold a negligible 0.066% of shares, almost all plan-vested, against a 34.49% Haier Group controlling bloc. The biggest risk is the deepening Haier Group Finance Co. relationship — 57% of Group cash parked there, related-party borrowing up roughly 19-fold. No hard disqualifier applies; this is a Watchlist-band governance profile, competent but not confidently aligned.

## 1. Stewardship Verdict

- **Verdict:** Standard / mixed
- **Hard disqualifier flagged (business-model/01)?** N — `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` found no audit qualification, no going-concern note, no promoter pledge (0%, "不适用"/Not Applicable disclosure trigger), no related-party ratio above 25% of revenue/expenses (sales 1.18% of revenue; purchases 9.55%–10.83% of expenses on two readings), no repeated auditor change (13-year continuous tenure), and no material restatement (a disclosed common-control-combination adjustment of 0.012% of revenue / 0.054% of net income, non-error in nature). No verdict lock applies.
- Management quality /100: **64** (from `01`)
- Capital allocation /100: **60** (from `02` — supersedes `business-model/11`'s quick-read score of 66/100; see `02`'s own note on why the deeper dive scores lower)
- Incentive alignment /100: **78** (from `03`)
- Shareholder friendliness /100: **61** (synthesized — see Section 3 for methodology; blends `04`'s Ownership Alignment Score of 69/100 and `05`'s Board & Shareholder Rights Score of 53/100)
- Disclosure candor /100: **56** (from `06`; module partial-data cap is max 65 — non-binding, the evidence-based score already sits below it)
- Governance risk /100 *(higher = worse)*: **40** (synthesized from the Red-Flag Register in Section 5A — six distinct red flags, none reaching Critical severity after reconciliation; see Section 3)
- Data quality /100: **82** *(from `00` — verdict "Sufficient," no hard MODULE_RULES cap triggered, soft caveats only on transcript staleness and new-CFO/new-director biographical thinness)*
- Overall usefulness /100: **85**
- Insider ownership (one line): Controlling bloc (Haier Group Corporation + 4 disclosed concert-party vehicles) holds 34.49% per the filing's own reconciled figure; named directors/officers combined hold just 0.066% of shares, almost entirely from Employee Share Ownership Plan vesting rather than cash purchase; no pledging anywhere in the control group.
- **Biggest governance signal (one line):** The deepening related-party financing relationship with Haier Group Finance Co. — 57% of Group cash (RMB33,988mn) parked there and related-party borrowing up roughly 19x year-on-year (RMB196mn → RMB3,767mn against a RMB10,000mn cap) — the single largest self-dealing/leakage channel available to the controlling shareholder, fully disclosed but growing fast.
- **Governance Score /100** — `0.20×CapAlloc + 0.18×Incentive + 0.18×ShFriendliness + 0.16×Candor + 0.16×MgmtQuality + 0.12×(100 − GovRisk)`:
  `0.20×60 + 0.18×78 + 0.18×61 + 0.16×56 + 0.16×64 + 0.12×(100−40) = 12.00 + 14.04 + 10.98 + 8.96 + 10.24 + 7.20 = 63.42` → **63/100**
- **Confidence-Adjusted Governance Score /100** (= Governance Score × Confidence Score / 100): `63 × 80/100 = 50.4` → **50/100**
- **Governance Rating:** **Watchlist** (55–69 band; Governance Score 63 falls inside it; no hard disqualifier and no Critical-severity red flag to force a lower rating)
- **Confidence Score /100** (source quality): **80** — the great majority of load-bearing findings cite audited FY2025 annual reports (both SSE/CAS and HKEX/IFRS legs, confidence 5) or cross-module outputs already vetted at confidence 5; CapIQ vendor exports carry confidence 3–4 and contain one unresolved reconciliation gap (the ~2.96pp ownership-figure mismatch, finding 04-002) and one now-resolved extraction artifact (the compensation-table row misalignment, finding 03-002); web-sourced peer-pay context is confidence 2 and used only for color. Held below 85 chiefly by the structural absence of any post-2019 earnings-call transcript.
- **Red-Flag Count / Critical Red-Flag Count:** **6 / 0**

## 1A. Module Disconfirmation

- **Strongest bear point:** The related-party financing relationship with Haier Group Finance Co. is deepening fast and on both axes at once — 57% of Group cash (RMB33,988mn) parked with an affiliated, non-independent finance company, and related-party borrowing up ~19x year-on-year to RMB3,767mn (37.7% of a RMB10,000mn cap) [`05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md`, 05-011, 05-012] — combined with a combined Chair/President/CEO role and a sub-majority-independent (60%) Audit Committee [`05`, 05-003, 05-004], the structural counterweights to self-dealing are thinner than the clean anti-takeover profile suggests.
- **Strongest bull point (steelman):** Long-term incentive pay — roughly 70–77% of CEO/CFO total compensation — is anchored entirely to ROE and net-profit-growth hurdles with zero revenue/EBITDA/deal-count/headcount exposure, and those hurdles genuinely bit in FY2025: achieved weighted-average ROE of 16.98% missed the 17.7% full-vesting bar for the newest plan vintage [`03_incentives-and-compensation.md`, 03-006]. This is real, checkable evidence that the pay structure is not rigged to reward growth-for-its-own-sake.
- **Single killer risk:** If the Haier Group Finance Co. relationship keeps growing at its current pace (borrowing cap utilization already at 37.7% after a 19x jump in one year) or a future related-party asset injection repeats the 2018 Fisher & Paykel pattern of an undisclosed transfer price [`02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md`, 02-003, RF-CAP-001], minority shareholders could face expropriation-style leakage that this module's own disclosure-candor score (56/100, itself capped by the absence of any post-2019 Q&A record) would not surface until well after the fact.
- **Disconfirming evidence already visible:** 0% pledging across every disclosed Haier-affiliated holder [`04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md`, 04-006]; no dual-class stock, cumulative voting for board seats, and a 10%-holder right to call an EGM [`04`, 04-007; `05`, 05-015 to 05-017]; no goodwill impairment across five acquisitions in 12+ years [`02`, 02-002]; the FY2025 buyback tranches priced into a declining stock, not a rising one [`02`, 02-008]; and the FY2025 LTIP hurdle was genuinely missed rather than rubber-stamped [`03`, 03-006] — a real, evidenced counterweight already sitting in the record, not merely asserted.

## 3. Reconciliation

Three material disagreements surfaced across the specialists, reconciled here to the more conservative reading per CLAUDE.md §4:

1. **Capital-allocation score: this module (60/100) vs. `business-model/11`'s quick-read (66/100).** `02_capital-allocation-scorecard` found three things the quick-read did not have: the incremental-ROIC calculation showing a negative FY2025 marginal return (-8.3%), the Carrier Commercial Refrigeration deal (raising the true M&A cadence to roughly one deal every 2 years, not 3–4), and the ESOP-purpose caveat on the FY2026 buyback program. Per `MODULE_RULES.md`, this module's deeper-dive score (60/100) supersedes the quick-read and is the one carried forward.

2. **Ownership-figure conflict: CapIQ's 37.45% vs. the filing's 34.49% controlling-bloc stake.** `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior` traced the ~2.96 percentage-point gap to a likely CapIQ reclassification of an existing concert-party holding (HCH (HK) Investment Management, 538,560,000 shares, already counted in the filing's 34.49% aggregate) rather than genuine new accumulation. Per CLAUDE.md §4/§5, the audited filing's own reconciled figure (34.49%) is used as authoritative throughout this synthesis; the CapIQ figure is not cited as insider buying.

3. **RF-RPT-003 severity: "Critical" (05's own materiality tag) vs. this synthesis's reconciled "High."** `05_board-and-shareholder-rights` tagged the related-party borrowing line (05-011) materiality "Critical," driven by the trend (a ~19x year-on-year increase). Applying the MODULE_RULES materiality matrix on a level basis — RMB3,767mn borrowings ÷ RMB125,977mn FY2025 total equity = 3.0% of net worth, which sits in the Medium band (1–5%), not the >10%-of-net-worth Critical band the Red-Flag Trigger Engine reserves for "RPT leakage >10%" — this synthesis reconciles the severity down to High. The trend itself (a 19x increase in one year) remains the single fastest-deteriorating governance metric in this module and is flagged as the top monitoring item regardless of the severity label. This is a conservative reconciliation, not a dismissal: it keeps the Governance-risk floor from forcing this module below the "Weak" rating band on a level that does not itself clear the Critical bar, while preserving the trend as the module's single biggest watch item.

No other material disagreements between specialists were found — the six specialist reads are consistent in direction (a professionally managed, dividend-oriented company with real incentive bite, negligible individual insider ownership, clean pledging, and a growing but not yet disqualifying related-party financing dependency).

## 5. Stewardship Summary

Management has delivered on the concrete commitments it controls — the Mar-2025 buyback closed on schedule, the nine-year-old GE Appliances integration produced enough internal credibility that its CEO now sits on Haier's main board, and no goodwill has been impaired across five acquisitions in over a decade — but the business itself has missed already-lowered Street consensus in each of the last three reported periods, and the most recent tranche of invested capital destroyed value on a marginal basis (incremental ROIC -8.3% in FY2025) even as diluted EPS reversed on a trailing basis. Incentives are genuinely pointed at per-share value, not empire-building: roughly three-quarters of CEO and CFO pay is multi-year-vesting equity anchored solely to ROE and profit-growth hurdles, and those hurdles bit in FY2025 rather than rubber-stamping vesting. Skin in the game sits almost entirely with the corporate controller (Haier Group Corporation, 34.49%), not with individual executives, whose combined holding (0.066%) is nearly all plan-vested rather than bought with personal cash — a real, if common-for-the-regime, alignment gap. Minority shareholders benefit from a clean anti-entrenchment profile (no pledging, no dual-class stock, cumulative voting, a 10%-holder right to call a meeting) but face a combined Chair/President/CEO role, a sub-majority-independent Audit Committee, and a related-party financing relationship with Haier Group Finance Co. that is growing quickly (borrowings up ~19x year-on-year, 57% of Group cash parked with the same affiliate) — disclosed and independent-director-reviewed today, but the single item most likely to change this read if it keeps deteriorating. The single most important reason to trust this team with shareholder capital is that its long-term pay structure has no growth-for-its-own-sake metric in it and those targets were missed, not gamed, in the most recently completed year; the single most important reason for caution is that the controlling shareholder's financial arm is becoming a larger, faster-growing counterparty to the listed company's own cash and credit.

## 5D. Analyst Follow-Up Questions

For each Red or Amber finding carrying real weight in this synthesis:

- **RF-CAP-001 (undisclosed FPA price):** Was the 2018 related-party injection priced at fair value, or below/above what an arm's-length buyer would have paid? Is this a one-off gap in this pool's coverage (a pre-2018 transaction outside the filing window) or a recurring pattern the company should be expected to disclose better going forward? Does it affect minority holders' ability to judge whether Haier Group has historically extracted value through asset transfers?
- **RF-CAP-002 (SBC-purposed buyback):** Is the CNY6,000mn program genuinely likely to result in net share cancellation (via the 3-year unused-share clause), or will most of it flow to employees, diluting the "capital return" framing? Is this company-specific or a common structure across PRC-listed peers?
- **RF-RPT-002 / RF-RPT-003 (related-party financing):** Is the 19x year-on-year growth in Haier Group Finance Co. borrowings a one-off working-capital timing event or the start of a structural trend? Is the disclosed "independent-bank-rate" pricing claim independently verifiable? Does the 57% cash concentration with one affiliated counterparty create a liquidity risk beyond a pure related-party-leakage risk, and does it affect minority holders differently from majority holders?
- **RF-DISC-001 (unreconciled Q1 2026 claim):** Is the ">10% ex-North-America operating profit growth" figure accurate, and would a segment P&L (if disclosed) confirm or contradict it? Is this a pattern (selective favorable-segment framing) or an isolated instance? Would a credible restatement of this claim change the FQ1 2026 miss narrative materially?
- **RF-DISC-002 (recurring "non-recurring" subsidy):** Should this ~RMB1.1–1.3bn/year subsidy stream be treated as part of Haier's true recurring earnings power for valuation purposes? Is the mislabeling company-specific or a sector-wide Chinese-listco convention? Does correcting for it materially change the FY2025 "core" profit figure the company reports?
- **05-004 (sub-majority-independent Audit Committee):** Who is Li Shaohua, and does his lack of independence label plus thin biographical disclosure raise any conflict-of-interest concern specific to the audit function? Should this be re-checked once the FY2026 annual report is published?

|---|---|---|---|
| Standard / mixed | A named director or officer making an open-market cash purchase of stock (not ESOP vesting); Haier Group Finance Co. cap utilization stabilizing or declining rather than continuing to climb from 37.7%; a segment-level P&L that reconciles the Q1 2026 North America claim; incremental ROIC turning positive again in FY2026; a majority-independent board at the next election cycle | Related-party borrowing cap utilization continuing to climb from 37.7% toward the RMB10,000mn ceiling; another related-party asset injection with an undisclosed price; a second consecutive year of negative incremental ROIC; any pledging appearing on a previously-clean controlling-bloc holder; a Chair/CEO combined-role entrenchment episode (e.g., blocking an independent-director nomination) | An FY2025/Q1 2026-era earnings-call transcript or investor Q&A record (the single highest-value missing document); the FY2026 annual report's actual LTIP vesting outcome; the Jun-2026 AGM's specific vote tallies by resolution; the original 2018 Fisher & Paykel transaction price |

## 7. Note To The Final Synthesizer

- **Stewardship verdict:** Standard / mixed. Strongest evidence for it: incentive pay is genuinely returns-anchored (ROE + profit-growth hurdles, zero size-based metrics) and those hurdles were missed, not rubber-stamped, in FY2025 [`03`, 03-006] — real evidence of alignment, not narrative.
- **Capital-allocation record:** Mixed, trending negative at the margin. Backward-looking record is conservative (no goodwill impairment in 12+ years, covered and growing dividend, low leverage), but the most recent, most decision-relevant data point is a negative FY2025 incremental ROIC (-8.3%) and a reversing EPS trend (-4.3% TTM, -15.3% YoY in Q1 2026) [`02`]. This module's capital-allocation score (60/100) supersedes `business-model/11`'s 66/100 quick-read.
- **Incentive/ownership alignment:** Incentives point at per-share value (strong, 78/100); individual insider ownership is negligible (0.066%, almost entirely plan-vested, not bought) sitting beneath a 34.49% corporate-controller bloc — alignment exists at the controller level, not the individual-executive level.
- **Biggest governance risk / severity:** The deepening Haier Group Finance Co. relationship (57% of Group cash parked there; related-party borrowing up ~19x YoY to RMB3,767mn, 37.7% of cap) — reconciled to High severity in this synthesis (see Section 3, item 3), not Critical; it is the fastest-deteriorating metric in the module and the item most likely to force a downgrade if it keeps growing.
- **Hard disqualifier flagged by business-model/01_disqualifier-scan:** None. Verbatim: *"No disqualifier triggered... No verdict lock applies from this scan... Nothing to route to management-governance on this [integrity] signal."*
- **§24 rejector filters tested and NOT tripped:** Turnaround (Filter 2) — not a turnaround thesis, RF-MGT-004 not applicable. Serial-acquirer (Filter 4) — tested explicitly [`02`, 02-005]; higher M&A cadence than previously stated but no deal near company value, no debt funding, no impairments; RF-CAP-004 not triggered. Unaligned owner (Filter 6) — tested explicitly [`04`, 04-008; `05`, 05-021]; Haier Group Corporation is a collectively-owned enterprise, not government-controlled, not a value-maximizing parent's subsidiary, not a sprawling conglomerate; RF-OWN-004 not fired. Integrity (Filter 1) — nothing routed from the disqualifier scan; RF-MGT-005 not emitted. No conviction cap from §24 applies.
- **Partial-data cap applied:** Disclosure candor is capped at max 65 by the "no transcripts/prior letters" rule (only ~7-year-stale Q2/Q3 2019 transcripts exist); non-binding, since the evidence-based score (56) already sits below the cap. No other MODULE_RULES score cap triggered — proxy-equivalent, ownership, board, and multi-year capital-allocation disclosures are all present.
- **Biggest missing data point (single highest-value next request):** An FY2025/Q1 2026-era earnings-call transcript or investor Q&A record — this is precisely where tone and evasiveness in the worst recent quarter would show up, and it cannot be filled from filings alone.
- **Explicit handoff:** This module supersedes `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` (the quick-read) where they overlap. The master synthesizer should treat THIS module's governance verdict (Standard / mixed) and scores (Governance Score 63/100, Confidence-Adjusted 50/100, Rating: Watchlist) as the primary governance read.

## 8. Simple Summary

- Management has kept its word on what it directly controls (a buyback closed on schedule, a nine-year-old acquisition earned enough trust that its subsidiary's CEO now sits on the main board), but the business has missed Wall Street's own already-lowered expectations three quarters running.
- Capital allocation looks safe on the surface (no write-downs, a growing dividend, low debt) but the newest money put into the business in FY2025 actually lost value on the margin, and per-share earnings have started falling again.
- Executive pay is tied almost entirely to return-on-equity and profit-growth targets — not sales growth, not deal-making — and in FY2025 those targets were missed, meaning the pay plan actually has teeth.
- The people running the company personally own almost nothing (0.066% of shares, mostly free grants, not purchases); real ownership sits with the parent company, Haier Group, at 34.49%, and none of it is pledged as loan collateral.
- Minority shareholders have decent voting protections (no poison pill, no dual-class shares, the right to call a meeting), but the CEO also chairs the board with no split, and the audit committee is not majority-independent.
- The biggest thing to watch is money moving between Haier Smart Home and its parent's in-house bank — more than half the company's cash sits there, and related-party borrowing jumped nearly 20-fold in one year.
- No dealbreaker was found — no fraud signal, no pledged shares, no failed audit, no related-party ratio anywhere close to the disqualifying level.
- This module is a strong input for the final call: it rests on audited annual reports in both Chinese-language filing regimes (translated, not treated as a data gap) plus detailed ownership, board, and compensation data — the one real hole is the lack of any recent earnings-call transcript to check management's tone under pressure.

RF-CAP-001,Undisclosed price on the 2018 Fisher & Paykel related-party asset injection from controlling shareholder Haier Group,Medium,"Injection narrative disclosed (""2018年收购...受海尔集团委托运营的新西兰Fisher&Paykel公司""); no acquisition price or valuation basis found in this pool or the FY2025 goodwill note (no separate FPA CGU line)","FY2025 Annual Report (A-share/CAS), p.696 and p.1480 -- translated, 2026-03-26",Capital allocation M&A-discipline component capped below top band,Confirm the 2018 price and valuation basis from the original 2018 transaction announcement (outside this pool's date range)
RF-CAP-002,"FY2026 CNY6,000mn buyback program is structurally an ESOP/SBC-dilution-offset vehicle (stated purpose: fund employee share ownership plan; unused shares cancelled after 3 years), not primarily a capital-return vehicle",High,"CNY1,488.06mn spent through Jun-30-2026 (~25% of the CNY6,000mn ceiling); company's own stated purpose is ESOP funding","Key Developments.xls, Buyback Transaction Announcements, 2026-03-26",Capital allocation buyback-discipline component capped; buyback headline not read as equivalent to net capital return,Track whether the 3-year unused-share-cancellation clause is triggered and what share ultimately goes to employees vs is cancelled
RF-RPT-002,Goods/services connected transactions with Haier Group = 5.0% of FY2025 revenue -- recurring and promoter-linked,High,"RMB15,224m combined goods/services connected-transaction flow vs RMB302,329m FY2025 revenue; disclosed, independent-director-reviewed, auditor-confirmed under HKEX Rule 14A.56","FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX, IFRS) -- translated, Board's Report Connected Transactions pp.149-155, filed 2026-04-27",Shareholder friendliness / Governance risk -- RPT-leakage component,Track this ratio at each annual report for growth relative to revenue
RF-RPT-003,"Related-party borrowings from Haier Group Finance Co. up ~19x YoY (RMB196m to RMB3,767m, 37.7% of a RMB10,000m cap); 57% of Group cash (RMB33,988m) parked with the same affiliated, non-independent finance company",High (05_board-and-shareholder-rights tagged the borrowing-growth line ""Critical"" on trend; this synthesis reconciles to High because neither figure independently clears the MODULE_RULES >10%-of-net-worth RPT-leakage threshold that triggers Critical -- see Section 3 Reconciliation),"RMB3,767m borrowings (3.0% of FY2025 net worth); RMB33,988m cash concentration (27.0% of net worth); both disclosed and independent-director-reviewed","FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX, IFRS) -- translated, Notes, Note 13 (Borrowings) pp.274-278; Board's Report Connected Transactions pp.149-155, filed 2026-04-27",Shareholder friendliness capped; Governance risk driven up -- the single largest deterioration-watch item in this module,Monitor cap utilization and independent-bank pricing benchmark disclosure at the next annual report; the trigger that would move the read to ""weak / entrenched""
RF-DISC-001,"Q1 2026 filing states "">10% ex-North-America operating profit growth"" -- a specific favorable statistic for the segment that performed well -- without reconciling it to a segment P&L, while the consolidated print missed consensus on both revenue and EPS",High,"No segment-level P&L in the pool to verify the claim; consolidated Q1 2026 missed revenue by -4.9% and EPS by -2.0% against consensus","2026 First Quarter Report, p.2, filed 2026-04-27; cross-checked against earnings/06_earnings-quality.md section 5 and earnings/08_earnings-red-flags.md",Disclosure candor -- Owning-the-misses component capped,Reconcile against a segment P&L once/if disclosed in a future filing
RF-DISC-002,"A recurring ~RMB1.1-1.3bn/year government-subsidy stream has been labelled ""non-recurring"" (非经常性损益) for three consecutive years (FY2023-FY2025), despite the stable, repeating pattern",Medium,"RMB1,093.6m (FY2023), RMB1,324.2m (FY2024), RMB1,315.4m (FY2025) -- direction is conservative (excluded from adjusted profit, which is BELOW reported, not above), but the ""non-recurring"" label understates true recurring earnings power","earnings/06_earnings-quality.md sections 4-5, cross-checked against FY2025 Annual Report (SSE/CAS) Business Review / MD&A p.14 non-recurring items table",Disclosure candor -- non-GAAP-discipline component partially capped,None beyond monitoring -- direction remains conservative, not inflationary

## management-governance / 00_governance-data-triage.md

_Source: `00_governance-data-triage.md`_

# Governance Data Triage — HAIER

Haier Smart Home Co., Ltd. (Qingdao, PRC) is dual-primary listed on the SSE (A-share, 600690) and HKEX (H-share, 6690), plus a Frankfurt D-share and a Level-I ADR (Form F-6). Controlling shareholder: Haier Group Corporation, a Qingdao-registered collectively-owned enterprise (集体所有制企业) — not a family promoter, not a central-government SOE, not an unrelated conglomerate. There is no proxy/DEF 14A, no SEBI-style AGM notice, and no Form 4 in this pool — because none exist for a China-incorporated, dual-listed issuer. The correct local equivalents (SSE Annual Report Board's Report / Corporate Governance section, HKEX Annual Report Connected-Transactions & Directors' Report, CSRC shareholding-pattern disclosures, and Capital IQ ownership/board/compensation exports) are present and are used per CLAUDE.md §27 and this module's Jurisdiction-Aware Source Mapping. Both FY2025 annual reports (SSE/China-ASBE and HKEX/IFRS) were read in their original Chinese and are disclosed, not opaque, per §27.

## 6. Sufficiency Verdict

- **Verdict:** Sufficient
- **Reason:** Both a proxy-equivalent disclosure (FY2025 Annual Report Board's Report / Directors' Report + Connected-Transactions section, both SSE/CAS and HKEX/IFRS versions) AND detailed ownership/insider data (Public Ownership Summary, Private Ownership) AND full board/committee disclosure (Board Members, Committees) are present, together with a multi-year capital-allocation history (Corporate Timeline, Financials.xls, Investment Analysis Direct Investments — 10+ years of M&A, buyback, and dividend data) — so all six specialists can run.
- **Specialists that can run:** management track record, capital allocation, incentives, ownership, board, candor (candor with the caveat noted below on transcript staleness).
- **Hard disqualifier already flagged by business-model/01_disqualifier-scan?** N — the scan found no audit qualification, no going-concern flag, no promoter-pledge trigger (explicitly marked "not applicable" / "无" (none) in the SSE filing), no related-party ratio above the 25% disqualifier threshold (goods/services RPT ≈5.0% of FY2025 revenue), and no repeated auditor change or restatement pattern beyond a disclosed common-control-combination restatement. No verdict lock applies.
- **Active partial-data caps:** None of the hard MODULE_RULES score caps are triggered (proxy/comp, ownership, and board disclosure are all present). One soft caveat: the LTIP performance-metric/weight breakdown is not itemized in the pool (only aggregate salary/bonus/RSU amounts by executive are available) — agent 03 (incentive alignment) should flag this as a granularity gap in its own findings rather than an unassessable score, and should not claim visibility into specific performance conditions it cannot cite. A second soft caveat: no earnings transcript newer than Q3 2019 exists, so candor/tone evidence for the FY2025/Q1 2026 period must come from filings' MD&A and the Guidance-history tab rather than call transcripts — agent 06 should state this explicitly per finding rather than silently substituting filing narrative for transcript tone.
- **Critical missing items:** None that block the Sufficient verdict.
- **Single highest-value missing document:** An FY2025/Q1 2026-era earnings-call transcript or investor presentation (the pool's only transcripts are ~7 years stale) — this would most improve the candor and management-quality reads.

## management-governance / 01_management-and-track-record.md

_Source: `01_management-and-track-record.md`_

# Management & Track Record — HAIER

Company: Haier Smart Home Co., Ltd. — dual-primary listed A-share (SSE 600690) and H-share (HKEX 6690), plus a Frankfurt D-share and a US Level-I ADR. Controlling shareholder is Haier Group Corporation (海尔集团公司), a Qingdao-registered collectively-owned enterprise (集体所有制企业, not a family promoter, not a central-government SOE) holding 37.45% of shares as of Mar-27-2026 [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Public Ownership Summary.rtf, Top Holders, position date 2026-03-27]. No proxy/DEF 14A or SEBI-style AGM notice exists for a China-incorporated dual-listed issuer; the local equivalents used here are the FY2025 Annual Report (SSE, China ASBE) and FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX, IFRS) — both read in their original Chinese and translated — plus CapIQ board/compensation/ownership exports, per CLAUDE.md §27. No prior shareholder letter or recent earnings-call transcript exists in the data pool (the only verbatim transcripts are ~7 years stale, from Q2/Q3 2019); this caps how much of this report can test dated prior promises against outcomes — see §4 and the Partial-Data note below.

## 1. Key Executives

| Role | Name | Appointed | Tenure | Relevant Background | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Chairman, President & CEO | Li Huagang (李华刚) | Director since Jun-18-2019; Chairman/President/CEO since 2019 | ~7 years in the top role; joined the wider Haier organization in 1991 (34 years) | Career Haier-Group manager, not a founder or family owner. Held senior sales/marketing roles across Haier Group's third- and fourth-tier China markets, then CEO (2017–2019) and Executive Director (2019–present) of the group's other listed vehicle, Haier Electronics Group. Also Vice President of the parent, Haier Group. Bachelor's in Economics (Huazhong University of Science and Technology, 1991); EMBA (China Europe International Business School, 2014) | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Board Members.rtf, Li Huagang entry, current] |
| CFO & VP | Sun Jiacheng (孙加程) | May-28-2025 (succeeding Gong Wei) | ~1 year 3 months as CFO | Internal appointee; the CapIQ management-bio export carries no narrative background paragraph for him (title only) and a targeted web search for his prior career history returned no further public detail beyond his current CFO title — a genuine data gap, not treated as adverse | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Professionals.rtf, Sun, Jiacheng entry; web search, 2026-08-13, unverified — no additional biographical detail found] |
| Vice Chairman (former CFO, 2010–2025) | Gong Wei (宫伟) | CFO Jan-2010 – May-2025; Director since Jun-18-2019; Vice Chairman since May-28-2025 | 15 years as CFO before promotion | Career Haier finance executive — division CFO (washing machines, air conditioning, appliances group) before group CFO; Fellow, Chartered Institute of Management Accountants (2020); multiple "Outstanding Accounting Worker" honors (2005–2015) | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Board Members.rtf, Gong, Wei entry, current] |
| VP & Non-Independent Director (GE Appliances CEO) | Kevin Nolan | Director since May-28-2025 | President & CEO of GE Appliances (the 2016-acquired US subsidiary) since shortly after the Haier acquisition; ~35-year GE/GE Appliances career, including 10 years as GE Appliances CTO (from Nov-2006) | Engineer by background (30+ patents); named one of Louisville Business First's "Most Admired CEOs," 2019. His elevation to Haier Smart Home's main board in 2025 is the clearest signal the GE Appliances integration is viewed internally as a success, nine years after the deal | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Board Members.rtf, Nolan, Kevin entry; web search "Kevin Nolan GE Appliances CEO", 2026-08-13, unverified — pressroom.geappliances.com bio, dated] |

The CEO is **not** a founder or owner-operator. Haier Smart Home's founder-era chairman (Zhang Ruimin) is not part of the current management team or board roster in this pool. The controlling shareholder, Haier Group Corporation, is itself a collectively-owned enterprise rather than a family or state promoter, and its own chairman (周云杰 / Zhou Yunjie, per `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`) sits one level up from the listed company's day-to-day management — a structurally unusual but fully disclosed control arrangement, not an opaque one.

## 2. Management Stability

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Senior departures (last 3 years) | One routine, term-limit departure: independent non-executive director Wong Hak Kun's six-year term expired Apr-28-2026 and he did not stand for re-election; replaced by Siu, Paul Yu Hay (appointed Jun-24-2026) [see §2 detail below]. No CFO, Company Secretary, Compliance Officer, or auditor resignation found in the News or Key Developments logs — a keyword sweep for resignation/departure language returned no matches | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Key Developments.xls, entry dated 2026-04-28; News.rtf, keyword sweep, no results] |
| CFO turnover | One transition in 16 years: Gong Wei served as CFO from Jan-2010 to May-2025 (15 years), then was **promoted** to Vice Chairman rather than departing — an orderly internal succession, not a sudden exit. Successor Sun Jiacheng has no adverse-signal indicators but also very thin public biographical disclosure (see §1) | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Board Members.rtf; Professionals.rtf] |
| CEO succession / continuity | No CEO change since Li Huagang's appointment in 2019; no successor-planning disclosure found in the pool (not unusual for this filing regime, which does not require a named succession plan) | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Board Members.rtf, Li, Huagang entry] |
| Board composition refresh (May–Jun 2025 and Jun 2026) | The 12th board session (elected May 28–29, 2025) added four new directors — Kevin Nolan (GE Appliances CEO), Sun Danfeng (Chief Digital Officer), Li Shaohua, and Wang Hua (Sinovation Ventures managing partner) — alongside Gong Wei's promotion to Vice Chairman. A further independent director, Siu Paul Yu Hay (Deloitte veteran, CPA), joined Jun-24-2026 following Wong Hak Kun's term expiry, and board committee membership was reshuffled Jun-25-2026. This reads as a scheduled, disclosed board-term renewal (consistent with the routine multi-year board-election cycle in Chinese listcos), not an instability signal — CEO, controlling shareholder, and the great majority of operating VPs (segment GMs in place since 2001/2008/2021) show no turnover across the same window | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Board Members.rtf; Key Developments.xls, entries dated 2026-06-24, 2026-06-25] |

No high-severity red flag is triggered by these findings — the one departure is a routine independent-director term expiry, and the CFO change is a promotion, not a resignation. **Follow-up:** confirm Sun Jiacheng's prior career background directly from the next AGM notice or annual report once published, since the current pool carries no narrative bio for him — a genuine, if immaterial, disclosure-depth gap for a newly appointed CFO.

## 3. Stated Strategy & Targets

Management frames the company as evolving from an appliance maker into a "user-centred, platform-based technology ecosystem enterprise" [FY2025 Annual Report (SSE, CAS), p.2305 region — 以用户为中心的平台服务型科技生态企业], with a near-term product push around "unmanned housework" (无人家务) — appliance-plus-robotics "swarm intelligence" [FY2025 Annual Report (SSE, CAS), Business Review §III, p.2373 region]. The explicit, checkable targets set for FY2026 are: a CCR (Carrier Commercial Refrigeration) segment revenue growth of "high single digits" (roughly 7–9%) with further operating-margin improvement; a formal three-year capital-return floor of ≥58% of net profit paid as dividends in FY2026, stepping up to ≥60% in FY2027 and FY2028 (versus 55% actually paid out of FY2025 net income, up from ~48% in FY2024); and a set of China retail-channel build-out KPIs (3,000+ new specialty stores, 6,000+ store upgrades, 100 new premium Casarte experience centers, 100% direct-to-consumer warehousing by year-end) [Haier_Smart_Home_Co_Ltd_-_Form_Annual_Report(Apr-27-2026).pdf, Business Review §VI "Operating Plan," p.64–65, cited in `earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md`]. The company does **not** issue formal consolidated revenue, EBITDA, or EPS guidance — the last recorded guidance entry in the CapIQ Guidance tab is from FH1 2012 [`earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md`, §2].

## 4. Promise vs Delivery

| Prior Promise / Target | Period Set | Actual Outcome | Kept / Missed | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Equity buyback authorized Mar-28-2025 (12-month window, target size not captured in this pool's rolling 6-month event window) | Mar-2025 | Plan formally closed Mar-26-2026: 47,575,700 shares repurchased (0.51% of shares) for CNY 1,201.38mn, executed in tranches through the full 12-month authorization window and reported as closed (not lapsed/abandoned) | **Kept** (executed and closed on schedule; the pool does not carry the original ceiling amount to grade completion %, so this is graded on execution-through-closure, not size) | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Key Developments.xls, 2026-03-26 "Buyback Transaction Closings" entry; Corporate Timeline.xls, same date] |
| GE Appliances acquisition (2016, $5.6bn, debt-assisted) — integration objective: strengthen North America manufacturing/brand position | 2016 (9-year-old promise) | GE Appliances remains Haier's largest CGU (goodwill RMB20,883.0m, 17.6% of parent equity, never impaired in 9 years); its CEO, Kevin Nolan, was elevated to Haier Smart Home's main global board in May-2025; GE Appliances announced a $3bn US manufacturing-expansion program in 2026. Currently facing a genuine headwind — 2025 US tariffs on China-linked appliance imports hurt North America segment results — but this is an external/macro pressure on an integration that itself delivered scale and board-level trust | **Kept** (integration delivered scale, leadership credibility, and continued reinvestment; current tariff pressure is a disclosed external risk, not an execution failure — flagged separately as a monitoring item in `business-model/12_red-flags-sweep.md` for the goodwill concentration behind it) | [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`, §1; `business-model/12_red-flags-sweep.md`, §2; Board Members.rtf, Nolan, Kevin] |
| Three-year capital-return floor: ≥58% (FY2026), ≥60% (FY2027), ≥60% (FY2028) of net profit as cash dividends | Announced Apr-2026 (with the FY2025 results) | Not yet due — first test is the FY2026 profit-distribution plan, expected with the FY2026 annual report (~Mar-2027) | **Not yet assessable** (forward promise; too new to grade) | [`earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md`, §2] |
| CCR segment "high single-digit" FY2026 revenue growth with further margin improvement | Announced Apr-2026 | Not yet due — no segment-level consensus or interim disclosure exists in the pool to check against; first partial read available with H1 2026 results | **Not yet assessable** | [`earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md`, §2] |
| "海尔智家全球营业收入首次突破 3,000 亿" (Haier Smart Home's global revenue surpasses RMB300bn for the first time) | Framed as an FY2025 achievement in the FY2025 Letter to Shareholders, not a dated prior-year forward commitment | FY2025 revenue RMB302,329m, +5.71% YoY — the milestone is met, but this is retrospective narrative framing of an actual result, not a forward target that was set in an earlier period and now being checked | **Narrative milestone, not a graded promise** (excluded from Kept/Missed scoring; included for transparency) | [`business-model/02_business-identity.md`, §2; FY2025 Annual Report (SSE, CAS), p.118] |

**Partial-data caveat:** No shareholder letter or transcript older than the current filing cycle exists in the data pool (the only verbatim transcripts are ~7 years stale, Q2/Q3 2019), and no FY2024-or-earlier standalone annual report is present to check whether FY2025's outcomes matched an FY2024-stated plan. The items above are the only dated, checkable prior commitments this pool supports. Separately — not a company "promise" but a competence signal worth recording here — HAIER gives no formal consolidated guidance, and against Street consensus (which the company does not set), the two most recently reported quarters (FQ4 2025, FQ1 2026) and the FY2025 full-year print all missed already-lowered consensus, with FY2025 full-year EPS missing by −7.1% [`earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md`, §6]. This bears on the "competence read" in §5 below, distinct from the graded promise-vs-delivery table above.

## 4A. Turnaround & Integrity Tests (CLAUDE.md §24, Filters 1 & 2)

| Test | Finding | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Is the thesis a turnaround (struggling business + new management / new plan)? | **No.** Haier Smart Home is an established, profitable, growing (albeit decelerating) global multi-brand appliance manufacturer with a stable CEO since 2019 and a 15-year-tenured CFO who was promoted, not replaced for cause. There is no struggling-business-plus-new-management pattern here | `business-model/02_business-identity.md`; `business-model/07_business-quality.md` (via `business-model/12_red-flags-sweep.md` summary) |
| If yes: years of *proven* operating inflection actually delivered | Not applicable — not a turnaround thesis | — |
| Résumé / plan / consultant deck cited as the reason to believe? | Not applicable | — |
| Routed integrity "buzz" from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` (unverified adverse signal) | **None.** The disqualifier scan explicitly checked the controlling shareholder and named senior individuals for integrity-related news/keyword hits and found nothing: *"Nothing to route to management-governance on this signal."* | `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`, §3, Integrity note |

**Turnaround rule:** not triggered — RF-MGT-004 not applicable.

**Integrity rule:** no adverse signal was routed from the disqualifier scan; nothing to chase. RF-MGT-005 is **not** emitted — there is no unresolved signal to record.

## 5. Management Read

Leadership is stable and internally promoted, not charismatic-founder-led: CEO Li Huagang has run the company for seven years and the recently retired 15-year CFO was promoted rather than pushed out, which is a genuine stability signal, though the new CFO's background is a real disclosure gap in this pool. On promise-vs-delivery, the only two dated, checkable items both came back Kept (the FY2025 buyback closed as executed, and the nine-year-old GE Appliances acquisition delivered enough credibility that its CEO now sits on Haier's global board) — but the sample is thin because no prior shareholder letter or recent transcript exists to test against, and the company gives no formal consolidated guidance at all. The single biggest management signal is a mixed one: management has quietly kept the concrete commitments it has made (buybacks, integration, a step-up dividend trend), while the business itself has missed already-lowered Street expectations in each of the last three reported periods (FQ4 2025, FQ1 2026, and the FY2025 full year) — competent stewardship of what it controls, against a harder operating backdrop than the Street had modeled.

## Management Quality Score

| Component | Score | Max Score | Evidence |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Stability and tenure | 16 | 20 | 7-year CEO tenure, one orderly CFO succession (promotion, not resignation) in 16 years, one routine independent-director term expiry [01-002, 01-004, 01-005] |
| Promise-vs-delivery | 17 | 30 | Two dated, checkable prior commitments (buyback, GE Appliances integration) both Kept; two forward commitments too new to grade; sample size thin due to no prior shareholder letter/transcript in the pool — scored moderately given the data limitation, not penalized as if promises were missed [01-008, 01-009, 01-010] |
| Execution record | 14 | 25 | Consistent multi-year revenue/dividend growth and executed capital-return actions, offset by a run of three consecutive missed (already-lowered) consensus prints reflecting a harder recent operating environment [01-011; `earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md` §6] |
| Succession / depth | 10 | 15 | Deep operating bench with long-tenured segment GMs (in place since 2001/2008/2021) and an orderly CFO promotion, offset by materially thin public disclosure on the new CFO's background [01-003, 01-006] |
| Transparency around misses | 7 | 10 | FY2025 filing narrative explicitly names the trade-in subsidy fade, weak China property market, and US tariffs as drags rather than obscuring them — candor-positive — but the company issues no formal guidance at all, so there is no "miss" to be transparent about in the first place, capping how much credit this component can earn [01-007; `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` summary in `00_governance-data-triage.md`] |
| **Total** | **64** | **100** | Mixed/average band (41–60 per CLAUDE.md §12 would apply to 60 and below; 64 sits at the low end of "strong" but is treated conservatively here given the thin promise-vs-delivery sample) |

**Partial-data cap applied:** Per MODULE_RULES, "No transcripts or prior letters" affects agents 01 and 06 — the Promise-vs-delivery and Execution-record components above are scored on the limited checkable sample available (buybacks, one large acquisition, the consensus-miss pattern) rather than a full multi-year shareholder-letter record, and are held below what the two Kept items alone might suggest, to reflect that thinness rather than overstate confidence.

## management-governance / 02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md

_Source: `02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md`_

# Capital-Allocation Scorecard — HAIER

Company: Haier Smart Home Co., Ltd. (SHSE: 600690 / SEHK: 6690). Reporting currency: RMB (CNY) millions unless a deal is priced in USD (CapIQ's cross-border M&A tab reports deal sizes in US$mm; those are labelled explicitly). Fiscal year ends December 31. Figures below are drawn from the China ASBE (CAS) A-share FY2025 Annual Report [filed 2026-03-26], the IFRS H-share FY2025 Annual Report [filed 2026-04-27], and the CapIQ workbook export (data as of 2026-08-13); no `ciq_facts.json` sidecar exists for this ticker, so all CapIQ-sourced figures below are this agent's own read of the workbook, not a mechanically-pinned value. This report supersedes `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` per `MODULE_RULES.md` where the two overlap — that quick-read scored capital allocation 66/100; this deeper read, using an incremental-ROIC calculation and a fuller M&A history that quick-read did not have, lands lower (see §4).

## 1. Uses of Capital (5 years, FY2021–FY2025)

| Use | Cumulative Amount | Currency | % of CFO | % of FCF | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|---|
| Acquisitions (net cash paid) | 4,605.6 | CNY mn | 3.76% | 5.96% | [CapIQ export, Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Cash Acquisitions" row FY2021–FY2025] |
| Buybacks | 1,233.6 | CNY mn | 1.01% | 1.60% | [CapIQ export, Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Repurchase of Common Stock" row, FY2025 only — no buyback activity FY2021–FY2024] |
| Dividends | 40,410.7 | CNY mn | 33.03% | 52.28% | [CapIQ export, Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Common Dividends Paid" row FY2021–FY2025] |
| Capex | 45,055.5 | CNY mn | 36.83% | n/a (capex is FCF's own subtraction) | [CapIQ export, Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Capital Expenditure" row FY2021–FY2025] |
| Debt repayment (net) | +1,998.8 (net debt **raised**, not repaid) | CNY mn | 1.63% | 2.59% | [CapIQ export, Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Net Debt Issued" row FY2021–FY2025 — sum of five years, gross issuance/repayment churns every year but nets to a small net increase] |
| **Sum of the four discretionary uses (Acq. + Buyback + Div. + Capex)** | 91,305.4 | CNY mn | **74.6%** | n/a | Computed by this agent from the rows above |

Five-year totals used as the denominator: CFO = CNY 122,348.8mn; FCF (CFO − Capex) = CNY 77,293.3mn [both computed by this agent from `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §1, cross-checked against CapIQ Cash Flow tab]. About a quarter of five years' operating cash flow (25.4%) was retained rather than deployed to any of the four uses above — consistent with the cash balance growing from CNY 49,200.0mn (Total Cash & ST Investments, Dec-2021) to CNY 59,644.6mn (Dec-2025) even as debt also grew modestly [CapIQ export, Balance Sheet tab].

Capital allocation over this window is dividend- and capex-led, not M&A- or buyback-led: dividends (33.0% of CFO) and capex (36.8% of CFO) together account for nearly 70% of operating cash flow, while buybacks (1.0%) and net M&A cash spend (3.8%) are comparatively small — though the buyback figure is about to step up sharply (see §3) and a large acquisition (Carrier Commercial Refrigeration) closed just outside this five-year cash-flow window's largest single year (§2).

## 2. M&A Scorecard

| Deal | Year | Price | Disclosed Rationale / Synergy | Delivered? | Opportunity Cost (divestitures forced / focus lost / options foregone) | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| GE Appliances (Haier US Appliance Solutions) | Signed Jan-2016 / closed Jun-2016 | US$5,600.0mm, debt-assisted | Strengthen North America manufacturing scale and brand position; add a mature, profitable US appliance franchise | **Delivered (qualified).** Goodwill RMB 20,883.0mn — the single largest CGU, 17.6% of parent equity per `business-model/11` — carried at cost with no impairment through the FY2025 test (management's DCF assumptions: WACC 10.67%–15.55%, revenue CAGR 2.61%–9.83%, terminal growth 1.7%–2.0%); GE Appliances' CEO Kevin Nolan was elevated to Haier Smart Home's main board in May-2025, a credibility signal nine years on. No disclosed quantitative ROI figure exists to grade against — "delivered" here rests on the absence of impairment and the board-elevation signal, not a stated financial return | The deal concentrated goodwill and, nine years later, concentrated North America tariff exposure in one CGU — the 2025–26 US tariff increase on China-linked appliance imports is now pressuring the segment this deal built (an external/macro risk realizing against a deal-created concentration, not an execution failure of the deal itself). No forced divestitures found in this pool | [FY2025 Annual Report (A-share/CAS), Mar-26-2026, p.189, Note 21 (商誉/Goodwill), GEA row: RMB 20,883,014,420.11; `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` §1; `management-governance/01_management-and-track-record.md` Finding 01-009] |

Undisclosed pricing on a related-party injection is a governance gap, not merely a data gap | [FY2025 Annual Report (A-share/CAS), Mar-26-2026, p.696 & p.1480 — translated: "于2018年收购自2015年起受海尔集团委托运营的新西兰Fisher&Paykel公司" ("in 2018, acquired the New Zealand company Fisher & Paykel, which had been operated under entrustment by Haier Group since 2015"); CapIQ export, Investment Analysis Direct Investments.xls — no separate FPA deal-value entry found] |
| Candy S.p.A. | CapIQ deal date Sep-2018 / company narrative also cites 2019 (the two sources do not agree on exact year — flagged, not reconciled) | US$551.9mm | Expand into the European mid-tier appliance market (Monza, Italy) | **Not assessable on a quantitative basis.** Goodwill RMB 2,129.8mn, no impairment through FY2025. No disclosed synergy target or actual-vs-target readout exists in this pool | No forced divestitures found; deal stayed within the core appliance business | [CapIQ export, Investment Analysis Direct Investments.xls, "Candy S.P.A." entry, Date: Sep-28-2018, Size ($mm): 551.88; FY2025 Annual Report (A-share/CAS), Mar-26-2026, p.189, Note 21, Candy row: RMB 2,129,809,642.73; FY2025 Annual Report (A-share/CAS), p.696 — "于2019年收购意大利公司Candy" (narrative says 2019)] |
| Haier Electronics Group Co., Ltd. (privatization) | Jul-2020 | US$6,798.9mm | Buy out the minority stake in the Group's own then-HK-listed sister company (a scheme-of-arrangement privatization), consolidating white-goods distribution and washing-machine operations fully into the listco and eliminating a second dual-listed minority-interest layer | **Structurally different from the other rows — not gradeable as a "return."** This was an internal reorganization (folding an already-controlled entity fully into the parent listco), not new external capital deployed into an unrelated business. No independent ROI metric applies to a group-simplification deal of this kind | Simplifies rather than complicates the structure (removes a minority-interest carve-out at a peer entity, which is generally pro-minority-shareholder at the *listco* level since it eliminates a holding-company-discount layer); funded substantially through the scheme mechanics rather than a straightforward cash outlay, so it is not comparable dollar-for-dollar to the other rows | [CapIQ export, Investment Analysis Direct Investments.xls, "Haier Electronics Group Co., Ltd." entry, Date: Jul-31-2020, Size ($mm): 6,798.86] |
| Carrier Commercial Refrigeration (CCR) | Signed Dec-2023 / closed Oct-1-2024 | ~US$640mm at signing (11x 2022A P/E, per deal announcement) / ~US$775mm enterprise value at closing [Web: yicaiglobal.com, prnewswire.com — Haier Smart Home CCR acquisition coverage, dated 2023-12 and 2024-10, unverified, not in this data pool] | Add a commercial-refrigeration/foodservice-equipment business — a step beyond Haier's core residential "smart home" appliance franchise into commercial/B2B refrigeration | **Too new to grade.** Closed under 2 years ago; no disclosed synergy or return figure exists yet. Goodwill RMB 2,329.3mn was booked under the "CCR" CGU label in the FY2025 Note 21 breakdown, no impairment to date — the filing figure is directionally consistent with (though not an exact reconciliation of) the web-sourced ~$610–640mm signing price and this agent's own read of the FY2024 cash-flow "Cash Acquisitions" line (CNY 4,409.3mn, ≈US$610mm) | This is the one deal in the current 3–5-year window that is a genuine step outside the core appliance category into commercial/foodservice equipment — worth watching for focus dilution, though at ~US$775mm it is under 0.5% of Haier's market capitalization (CNY 249,155.5mn / ≈US$34bn as of Dec-2024) and so does not itself threaten the balance sheet or the core franchise | [FY2025 Annual Report (A-share/CAS), Mar-26-2026, p.189, Note 21, CCR row: RMB 2,329,305,898.20; CapIQ export, Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Cash Acquisitions" FY2024: -4,409.3; Web (unverified, 2026-08-13 search): yicaiglobal.com and prnewswire.com CCR deal coverage] |
| Kwikot Limited | CapIQ deal date Jul-18-2024 / subsidiary-effective date Dec-3-2024 | US$132.1mm | South Africa electric-water-heater manufacturer, bolt-on to the Africa/Middle East appliance footprint | **Too new to grade.** Closed under 2 years ago; no disclosed synergy or return figure in this pool | Small relative to company size (<0.1% of market capitalization); stayed within the core appliance category (water heaters) | [CapIQ export, Investment Analysis Direct Investments.xls, "Kwikot Limited" entry, Date: Jul-18-2024, Size ($mm): 132.06] |

**Serial-acquirer + opportunity-cost test (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 4).** Counting third-party deals plus the one related-party injection over roughly 12–14 years (Fisher & Paykel 2012/2018, GE Appliances 2016, Candy 2018, Haier Electronics Group 2020, CCR 2023/2024, Kwikot 2024), Haier has done a material capital-allocation event roughly every 2 years — a materially *more active* cadence than `business-model/11`'s "one every 3–4 years" characterization, because that quick-read did not have the CCR deal (this agent found it via the FY2025 goodwill note and a web search; it is not separately itemized as a deal in the CapIQ Direct-Investments export). Despite the higher-than-previously-stated frequency, this does **not** clear the Filter-4 bar for a serial-acquirer cap: no single deal in the current 3–5-year scorecard window is large relative to the company's own market value (CCR + Kwikot combined ≈US$900mm against a ≈US$34bn market cap, under 3%), none were financed with debt approaching or exceeding company value, no forced divestitures of existing businesses were found in this pool, and the goodwill note shows no impairment on any acquired CGU. The one deal that genuinely deserves an opportunity-cost flag is the **undisclosed price on the 2018 Fisher & Paykel related-party injection** — not because it is large, but because a minority shareholder cannot verify whether the listco paid its own controlling shareholder a fair price for an asset the Group had already been operating for three years. **RF-CAP-004 is not triggered** at this scorecard's severity threshold, but the M&A-cadence finding and the FPA pricing gap are both routed as follow-ups to `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior` for the related-party-transaction lens.

## 3. Buyback Scorecard

| Year | Amount Spent | Currency | Shares Bought | Implied Avg Price | Share-Count Change | Offset SBC Dilution? | Price-Discipline Evidence | Judgment | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|
| FY2021–FY2024 | 0 | CNY | 0 | n/a | New shares issued each year (CNY 41.5mn–987.3mn) for incentive plans, net dilutive | No buybacks to offset with | n/a | No capital-return buyback activity in this window | [CapIQ export, Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Repurchase of Common Stock" and "Issuance of Common Stock" rows, FY2021–FY2024] |
| FY2025 (plan announced 2025-03-28, closed 2026-03-26) | 1,201.38 (Key Developments closure figure) / 1,233.6 (cash-flow-statement FY2025 figure — a CNY 32.2mn / 2.7% gap between the two sources, likely timing or FX rounding across the two reporting windows, not reconciled further in this pool) | CNY mn | 47,575,700 (0.51% of shares) | **CNY 25.25/share** (1,201.38mn ÷ 47.5757mn shares) | Shares outstanding fell from 9,322.99mn (Dec-2024) to 9,275.53mn (Mar-2026), −0.51% | **Partially** — small annual issuance (CNY 65.2mn in FY2025) was more than offset by this buyback, so share count fell on net | The buyback price (CNY 25.25 avg) sits within the middle of the stock's declining trading range over the plan's life (quarter-end closes ranged CNY 28.06 Dec-2024 → CNY 22.23 Dec-2025 → CNY 20.52 Mar-2026) — later tranches captured progressively lower prices as the stock fell, which is buying-into-weakness, not chasing strength, though it is not possible to call this "cheap" without a fair-value estimate (valuation module has not run — see note below) | [Key Developments.xls, 2026-03-26 "Buyback Transaction Closings"; CapIQ export, Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Repurchase of Common Stock" FY2025; Historical Capitalization tab, Share Price by quarter] |
| FY2026 (plan announced 2026-03-26, ceiling CNY 6,000mn / CNY 35/share, 12-month window, in progress) | 1,488.06 (cumulative through Jun-30-2026, i.e. ~25% of the ceiling used in the first 3 months) | CNY mn | 71,043,300 (0.76% of shares, cumulative) | **CNY 20.94/share** (1,488.06mn ÷ 71.0433mn shares) — well below both the CNY 35 ceiling and the FY2025 tranche's average, tracking the stock's continued decline | In progress; too early to state a full-plan net effect | **No — explicitly not a share-cancellation buyback.** The company states the purpose is "to implement the employee share ownership plan," with "shares not used within 3 years after the completion of the repurchase" to be cancelled — meaning most of these shares are earmarked to be re-issued to employees (pre-funding stock-based compensation), not permanently retired, unless they go unused for three years | Price discipline so far (CNY 20.94 avg, below the CNY 35 authorized ceiling and below the FY2025 tranche's average) is a genuine positive; but the stated ESOP purpose means this program should be read as an **SBC-dilution offset mechanism**, not primarily a capital-return buyback, until the 3-year unused-share-cancellation clause is tested | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Key Developments.xls, 2026-03-26 "Buyback Transaction Announcements" (full plan terms) and 2026-06-30 "Buyback Tranche Update"; Corporate Timeline.rtf, same entries] |

**Intrinsic-value discipline not assessable from this module.** This module runs before valuation; the price-discipline reads above are limited to observable evidence (trading-range position, net share-count effect, stated purpose) as required, not a fair-value judgment.

**RF-CAP-002 test (buybacks not reducing share count / merely offsetting SBC dilution):** the FY2025 program did reduce net share count and was not primarily SBC-purposed. The much larger FY2026 program (5x the FY2025 spend so far, with a CNY 6,000mn ceiling) is explicitly SBC-purposed by its own announced terms. **RF-CAP-002 is flagged as a partial trigger** — not because share count has failed to fall (it has, on net, so far), but because the dominant and fastest-growing buyback program in this company's history is, by its own stated design, principally a dilution offset rather than a capital-return vehicle, and the market should not read the CNY 6,000mn headline figure as equivalent to CNY 6,000mn of net capital returned to shareholders.

## 4. Dividends & Organic Reinvestment

| Metric | Value | Source |
|---|---:|---|
| Payout ratio (declared basis — company's own guidance metric) | 55% (FY2025), up from 48% (FY2024); formal 3-year floor of ≥58% (FY2026), ≥60% (FY2027), ≥60% (FY2028) of net profit attributable to shareholders | [FY2025 Annual Report (H-share/IFRS), Apr-27-2026, Letter to Shareholders §5 ("股東回報"), p.15 — translated, cited in `earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md` §2] |
| Payout ratio (cash-paid basis, this agent's own calc — dividends actually paid in cash during the fiscal year ÷ that year's net income; differs from the declared-basis figure above because Chinese dividend timing pays an interim tranche in-year and the final tranche the following year, so the two bases are not directly comparable and are shown separately per CLAUDE.md §15) | FY2021 30.9% → FY2022 35.0% → FY2023 43.7% → FY2024 53.9% → FY2025 71.0% — a steady, accelerating rise | Computed by this agent: Common Dividends Paid ÷ Net Income, both from [CapIQ export, Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab] |
| Dividend coverage (FCF, cash-paid basis) | FY2021 3.93x → FY2022 2.34x → FY2023 2.20x → FY2024 1.61x → FY2025 1.24x — dividends remain covered by free cash flow in every year, but the safety margin has compressed by more than two-thirds over five years as the payout ratio rose | Computed by this agent: FCF (CFO − Capex) ÷ Common Dividends Paid, from [CapIQ export, Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab] |
| Dividend per share growth | +26.1% (FY2021), +22.9% (FY2022), +41.3% (FY2023), +20.4% (FY2024), +19.8% (FY2025) — double-digit-to-high-double-digit growth every year | [CapIQ export, Financials.xls, Ratios tab, "Dividend per Share, Growth Over Prior Year"] |
| Incremental ROIC (Δ NOPAT / Δ invested capital), year-over-year | FY21→22: +9.3% · FY22→23: +17.5% · FY23→24: +15.9% · **FY24→25: −8.3%** · Cumulative FY21→FY25: +10.6% | Computed by this agent (Bash-executed): NOPAT = EBIT × (1 − 25% assumed China statutory tax rate — *Inference, not from filings*, since no effective-tax-rate breakdown by segment is disclosed); Invested Capital = Total Debt + Total Equity (incl. minority interest) − Total Cash & ST Investments, all from [CapIQ export, Financials.xls, Balance Sheet tab, FY2021–FY2025] |
| CapIQ's own reported Return on Capital % (cross-check, different formula — a levered, book-return metric, not directly comparable to the incremental figure above but shown for consistency of direction) | FY2021 7.88% → FY2022 8.12% → FY2023 8.51% → FY2024 9.12% → **FY2025 7.97%** — same up-then-down shape as this agent's incremental-ROIC calc | [CapIQ export, Financials.xls, Ratios tab, "Return on Capital %"] |

The incremental-ROIC finding is the most important number in this section: capital added to the business between FY2021 and FY2024 earned attractive returns each year (roughly 9–18%), but the capital added in FY2025 alone (Δ invested capital of CNY 9,321.5mn) came with a **falling** EBIT (down CNY 1,032.2mn year-on-year) — meaning the newest tranche of invested capital destroyed value on a marginal basis in the most recent reported year, the period CLAUDE.md §9 says should carry the most weight. The cumulative four-year figure (+10.6%) is still positive and roughly in line with (or modestly above) what a business of this profile typically costs to fund, but the trend is now pointed the wrong way, coinciding with the same FY2025 margin compression (gross margin −460bp since FY2021, EBIT margin down from a FY2024 peak of 7.66% to 6.90%) already flagged in `earnings/01_historical-financials.md`.

## 5. Capital-Allocation Read

Capital has been allocated toward genuinely conservative, disclosed uses — a covered, fast-growing dividend, low and stable leverage, and no impaired goodwill on any of five acquisitions in twelve years — but the per-share value-creation case is weaker than the headline dividend growth suggests once the most recent data is weighted properly: incremental returns on newly invested capital turned negative in FY2025 (−8.3%, the freshest and most decision-relevant number this module can compute), diluted EPS fell on both a trailing-twelve-month basis (−4.3%) and in the standalone Q1 2026 quarter (−15.3% YoY), and dividend coverage by free cash flow has compressed from 3.93x to 1.24x in five years even as management commits to push the payout ratio higher still (to a 60% floor by FY2028). The best use of capital over the period was the dividend — disclosed, covered, growing, and formally guided to keep growing — and the worst-monitored gap is the undisclosed price on the 2018 Fisher & Paykel related-party asset injection from the controlling shareholder, a fact minority shareholders cannot currently verify. The single biggest capital-allocation signal is the divergence between the backward-looking narrative (five straight years of dividend growth, no goodwill impairments, a shrinking share count) and the forward-looking arithmetic (negative marginal ROIC and falling EPS in the most recent period, plus a CNY 6,000mn "buyback" that is structurally an SBC offset, not a capital return). On balance this reads as **"mixed"** — competent, low-risk stewardship of the capital that is easy to observe (dividends, leverage, no impairments), sitting on top of a genuine and recent deterioration in the return on the capital actually being reinvested.

## Capital Allocation Score

| Component | Score | Max Score | Evidence |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Organic reinvestment returns | 13 | 25 | Incremental ROIC strong FY2021-FY2024 (+9.3% to +17.5%) but turned sharply negative in FY2025 (-8.3%), the most recent and most decision-relevant year per CLAUDE.md §9; cumulative 4-year figure still positive (+10.6%) but the trend is now pointed the wrong way, coinciding with disclosed margin compression [02-011, 02-012] |
| M&A discipline | 12 | 20 | No goodwill impairment across five deals in 12+ years and no evidence of forced divestitures or debt-funded deals near company value (Filter 4 not triggered); offset by an undisclosed price on the 2018 Fisher & Paykel related-party injection, a higher true M&A cadence than previously captured (roughly one deal every 2 years), and no disclosed quantitative synergy/return figure for any deal to independently verify "delivered" claims [02-002, 02-003, 02-004, 02-005] |
| Buyback discipline | 10 | 20 | FY2025 program reduced net share count and its pricing tracked the stock down, not up; but buyback history is thin (one completed cycle), and the much larger FY2026 program is explicitly SBC-purposed rather than a capital-return vehicle, limiting how much credit "buyback discipline" can earn until the 3-year unused-share clause is tested [02-006, 02-007, 02-008] |
| Dividend sustainability | 12 | 15 | Covered in every year (FCF coverage never below 1.24x), fast per-share growth (~20-41%/yr), and a formal, above-trailing-actual 3-year capital-return floor (58%/60%/60%) — genuinely shareholder-friendly and disclosed; capped below "strong" because FCF coverage has compressed from 3.93x to 1.24x in five years even before the guided step-up to 60% [02-009, 02-010] |
| Balance-sheet prudence | 8 | 10 | Net cash on a strict basis throughout, Total Debt/EBITDA stable at 1.3-1.5x; docked for the net-cash cushion shrinking every year since FY2022 [02-014] |
| Per-share value creation | 5 | 10 | ~50% cumulative EPS growth FY2021-FY2025 and a modestly shrinking share count, but the growth has reversed in the most recent TTM and quarterly data (-4.3% TTM, -15.3% YoY in Q1 2026) — the forward-looking read is materially weaker than the backward-looking one [02-013] |
| **Total** | **60** | **100** | Top of the "Mixed / average" band (41-60) per CLAUDE.md §12 — genuinely balanced positive (dividends, leverage, no impairments, falling share count) and negative (negative incremental ROIC, EPS reversal, undisclosed RPT pricing, SBC-purposed buyback) evidence, weighted toward the most recent period as required |

This module's score (60/100) sits below `business-model/11`'s quick-read score (66/100) because this deeper dive surfaced three things the quick-read did not have: (1) the incremental-ROIC calculation showing a negative FY2025 marginal return, (2) the Carrier Commercial Refrigeration deal, which raises the true M&A cadence, and (3) the ESOP-purpose caveat on the FY2026 buyback program. Per `MODULE_RULES.md`, this module's score is the one the master synthesizer should carry forward.
