## management-governance / 03_incentives-and-compensation.md

_Source: `03_incentives-and-compensation.md`_

# Incentives & Compensation — HAIER

## 1. Compensation Structure

Reporting currency: **CNY (RMB)**. Fiscal year = calendar year (Jan 1 – Dec 31). Figures below are FY2025 (year ended Dec-31-2025) unless stated, sourced from the HKEX-format FY2025 Annual Report's Directors' Remuneration note and Share Award Plan disclosures [FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX, translated), Directors' Report §"董事薪酬" p.121, and Share Award Plan §"股份獎勵計劃" pp.126–146], cross-checked against the CapIQ compensation export. **Note on the CapIQ export**: the raw RTF table's numeric-value blocks and their text labels ("Salary"/"Bonus"/"All Other Compensation"/"Total Annual Cash Compensation") were misaligned by the RTF-to-text extraction (a bad-extraction artifact per CLAUDE.md §20). This was resolved by arithmetic cross-validation — the reconstructed Salary+Bonus+All Other figures sum exactly to the disclosed Total Annual Cash Compensation, and Total Cash + Restricted Stock sum exactly to the disclosed "As Reported Total Executive Compensation," for all three individuals tested (CEO, outgoing CFO, VP Nolan). The figures below use the reconciled values, not the raw (mislabeled) row order.

| Executive | Base (Salary) | Annual Bonus (cash) | Other Cash | Long-Term Incentive (equity, fair value at grant) | Total | Cash vs Equity Mix | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| CEO — Li Huagang (李華剛), Chairman/President/CEO | CNY 2,648,000 | CNY 900,000 | CNY 238,000 | CNY 12,967,000 (2025-vintage A- & H-share Core Employee Stockholding Plan grant, restricted-stock-equivalent) | CNY 16,753,000 | 22.6% cash / 77.4% equity | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Compensation Summary Compensation.rtf; FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX), Note 8, reconciled] |
| CFO (outgoing) — Gong Wei (宮偉), CFO Jan-2010–May-2025, final full year FY2024 | CNY 1,491,000 | CNY 650,000 | CNY 153,000 | CNY 5,386,000 | CNY 7,680,000 | 29.9% cash / 70.1% equity | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Compensation Summary Compensation.rtf, reconciled] |
| CFO (incoming) — Sun Jiacheng (孙加程), CFO since May-2025 | Not broken out | Not broken out | Not broken out | Not broken out | CNY 1,070,000 (FY2025, partial year, "As Reported Total Executive Compensation" only) | Not disclosed | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Compensation Summary Compensation.rtf] |

**Context flag (not required by report structure, but material to the alignment read):** the single highest-paid individual in the entire disclosed compensation table is **not** the CEO — it is Kevin Nolan, VP & President/CEO of GE Appliances (the US subsidiary), at CNY 23,179,000 total FY2025 (Salary CNY 12,012,000 + Bonus CNY 7,499,000 + Other CNY 361,000 + Restricted Stock CNY 3,307,000). Haier's disclosure does not break out what performance metrics govern Nolan's pay — it is reasonable to infer his cash bonus follows GE Appliances' own US incentive plan rather than the China-listco ROE/profit-growth formula described in §2 below, but this cannot be confirmed from the pool. Flagged as a disclosure gap, not treated as an adverse finding [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Compensation Summary Compensation.rtf, reconciled].

## 2. Performance Metrics

The **annual cash bonus** for executive directors has **no disclosed formula or weighted metrics** — the Remuneration & Assessment Committee ("薪酬與考核委員會") states only that pay "will also make reference to the Company's performance, current market conditions, and each executive director's performance or contribution," and that discretionary bonuses are set with reference to peer-company pay and each individual's contribution to Group objectives, without a published scorecard [FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX), Corporate Governance Report §"薪酬與考核委員會之主要職責", p.94]. This is a genuine disclosure gap on the smaller, cash portion of pay.

The **long-term incentive** — which is ~70–77% of CEO/CFO total pay — has an unusually well-disclosed, explicit metric structure, set out per plan vintage:

| Plan | Metric | Weight | Threshold(s) | Per-Share/Returns or Size-Based? | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Annual cash bonus (all vintages) | Discretionary — no published metric/weight; "company performance, market conditions, individual contribution" | Not disclosed | Not disclosed | **Cannot classify — undisclosed formula** | [FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX), p.94] |
| A/H-Share Core Employee Stockholding Plan, 2023 & 2024 vintages (covers CEO, President, Supervisors, "platform" staff) | (a) 扣非歸母淨利潤複合增長率 — net profit attributable to parent, excluding non-recurring items, compound annual growth rate vs. base year | 50% | ≥15% CAGR for full-weight credit; completion ratio = actual CAGR ÷ 15% | **Returns/growth-based** (earnings growth, not a pure per-share metric, but not revenue/EBITDA/size either) | [FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX), pp.129–133, Notes 1–2 to Share Award Plan] |
| Same plan, same vintages | (b) 加權平均淨資產收益率 (ROE) — weighted-average return on equity, excluding capital-raising and M&A distortions | 50% | ≥16.8% for full-weight credit; completion ratio = actual ROE ÷ 16.8% | **Returns-based** | [same source] |
| A/H-Share Core Employee Stockholding Plan, 2025 vintage (directors/President/platform staff only) | ROE (weighted-average, ex-refinancing/M&A/disposal effects) — sole metric | 100% | FY2025: ROE ≥17.7% → full (40%) vesting of first tranche; FY2026: 16.8%–17.7% → partial vesting at Committee discretion; <16.8% → forfeiture | **Returns-based** | [FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX), pp.134–135] |
| A-Share Stock Option Plan (2021 vintage, 5 annual tranches 2021–2025) | 歸母淨利潤增長率/複合增長率 — net profit attributable to parent, growth vs. base year (2020, adjusted) | 100% | Yr1: ≥30% growth; Yrs2–5: ≥15% CAGR vs. base year, each tranche 20% of grant | **Growth-based** (not per-share, not size/absolute; a % hurdle on parent-attributable profit) | [FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX), pp.144–145] |
| A-Share Stock Option Plan (2022 vintage, 4 annual tranches 2022–2025) | Same metric family, ≥15% CAGR each year vs. 2021 base, each tranche 25% of grant | 100% | ≥15% CAGR | **Growth-based** | [same source] |

**No revenue, absolute EBITDA, deal-count, or headcount metric appears anywhere in the disclosed LTI structure.** Every quantitative long-term hurdle is either ROE (a returns metric) or a % growth rate on net profit attributable to parent shareholders (an earnings-growth metric, not an absolute-size metric) — this is a meaningfully different, more shareholder-aligned design than a typical revenue- or EBITDA-anchored plan. The weak point is the annual cash bonus, which carries no published formula at all.

## 3. Pay vs Performance

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| CEO total pay (latest, FY2025) | CNY 16,753,000, up from CNY 15,064,000 in FY2024 (+11.2% YoY) | [Compensation Summary Compensation.rtf, reconciled] |
| Company performance (same period) | Net profit attributable to parent shareholders +4.4% YoY (CNY 19,553m FY2025 vs CNY 18,731m FY2024, restated); revenue +5.71% YoY | [FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX), Note 12 (EPS); `business-model/02_business-identity.md` §2] |
| FY2025 achieved ROE vs. LTIP hurdle | Company-disclosed weighted-average ROE (加權平均淨資產收益率) = **16.98%** for FY2025, down from 17.61% in FY2024 (−0.63pp) — this **misses** the 17.7% full-vesting bar set for the 2025-vintage Core Employee Stockholding Plan's first tranche, and sits inside the 16.8%–17.7% partial/discretionary-vesting band the plan defines for its second (FY2026) test year; if the same band logic applies to the FY2025 test (consistent with the 2023/2024-vintage plans' explicit 0.8–1.0 "completion ratio → Committee discretion" rule), FY2025 performance likely triggered a reduced, not full, vesting outcome for that tranche — a real miss against the plan's own top hurdle, not a rubber-stamped target | [Haier_Smart_Home_Co_Ltd_-_Form_Annual_Report(Mar-26-2026).pdf, financial-highlights table, p. per "加权平均净资产收益率（%）16.98 / 17.61"; cross-checked against CapIQ Ratios: Return on Common Equity 16.97% FY2025 vs 17.58% FY2024] |
| FY2025 profit growth vs. LTIP hurdle | Reported (unadjusted) net profit attributable to parent grew +4.4% YoY — far short of the 15% CAGR hurdle required by the 2024-vintage plan's FY2025 tranche and the 2021/2022 stock-option plans' final tranches. **Caveat:** the plans test 扣非歸母淨利潤 (net profit excluding non-recurring items), not the headline reported figure used here, and the pool does not carry that adjusted series or the exact 2023 base-year figure needed for a precise CAGR — so this is a directional, not exact, read, and the residual gap between the ~+4% reported growth and the required 15% CAGR is the finding, not a precisely computed miss | [FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX), Note 12; pp.129–133 (plan hurdles); analyst calculation, flagged as approximate per CLAUDE.md §15] |
| Pay vs. domestic peers (web-sourced, unverified) | Haier CEO total pay (CNY 16.75m) sits between Gree Electric's Chairman Dong Mingzhu (CNY ~12.93m, cash-only, pre-tax, FY2025) and Midea Group's CEO Fang Hongbo (CNY ~81.89m, 16.8% salary / 83.2% bonus+equity, FY2025) — Haier's CEO pay is materially lower than Midea's despite comparable or larger revenue scale, and its cash:equity mix (~23:77) is closer to Midea's equity-heavy structure than to Gree's cash-only structure | [Web search, 2026-08-13, unverified, dated — no primary-filing citation available for peer figures] |
| Say-on-pay / shareholder vote opposition | China listcos have no binding "say-on-pay" vote equivalent to a US DEF 14A advisory vote. Equity incentive plans (Core Employee Stockholding Plans, RSU plans, stock-option plans) do require a ≥66.67% supermajority shareholder resolution at an AGM/EGM to adopt, with related-party/interested shareholders required to abstain — a real minority-protection mechanism — but the pool does not carry the specific for/against vote tallies or any recorded opposition percentage for the 2025-vintage plans | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Takeover-Defenses.xls, Corporate Governance sheet, "% of Shareholder Approval Required to Amend Bylaws / Supermajority = 66.67%"; FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX), Share Award Plan section — Insufficient Data on the specific vote count] |
| Aggregate equity-incentive cost | Total FY2025 expense recognized for the A- & H-Share Core Employee Stockholding Plans alone = CNY 868m, ≈4.4% of FY2025 net profit attributable to parent (CNY 19,553m) — a moderate, not outsized, dilution/expense load | [FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX), p.136] |
| Non-executive/independent director pay | Fixed fee CNY 360,000/year plus committee-role fees (CNY 20,000–30,000/year) and per-diem attendance allowances (CNY 5,000/day) — a modest, non-performance-linked structure appropriate for oversight roles; non-executive directors employed by the Haier Group parent receive no separate director fee from the listco (avoids double-dipping) | [FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX), p.121, "非執行董事薪酬"] |

## 4. Incentive Alignment Read

Haier Smart Home's long-term incentive plans — which make up roughly three-quarters of CEO and CFO total pay — are anchored entirely to ROE (a returns metric) and net-profit-growth hurdles (an earnings-growth metric), with zero exposure to revenue, absolute EBITDA, deal count, or headcount anywhere in the disclosed structure; the FY2025 results show the top ROE hurdle (17.7%) was missed (actual 16.98%) and reported profit growth (+4.4%) fell well short of the 15% CAGR bar embedded in two option/stockholding-plan vintages, evidence these targets bite rather than rubber-stamp vesting. The weak spot is the annual cash bonus — roughly a fifth to a third of executive pay — which carries no published metric or weight at all, just board/remuneration-committee discretion referencing "company performance and individual contribution," and the fact that the single highest-paid individual in the whole table (GE Appliances CEO Kevin Nolan, CNY 23.2m) is paid more than the Haier Smart Home CEO under an incentive structure this disclosure does not detail. The single biggest incentive signal is that Haier's equity incentive design has no growth-for-its-own-sake metric in it at all — it pays out on ROE and profit growth, and in FY2025 those hurdles were genuinely missed rather than gamed. **Conclusion: well-aligned (returns/per-share)**, with two flagged gaps (undisclosed cash-bonus formula; undisclosed metrics for the highest-paid individual) that keep this from a stronger rating.

## management-governance / 04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md

_Source: `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md`_

# Ownership & Insider Behavior — HAIER

Company: Haier Smart Home Co., Ltd. — dual-primary listed A-share (SSE 600690) and H-share (HKEX 6690), plus a small Frankfurt D-share tranche and a US Level-I ADR. Total share capital at 2025-12-31 was 9,377,629,650 shares (6,253,028,411 A shares = 66.68%, 271,013,973 D shares = 2.89%, 2,853,587,266 H shares = 30.43%) [FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX, IFRS, Traditional Chinese) — translated, Directors' Report "董事及行政總裁於股份及相關股份之權益及淡倉", p.121-122]. No US-style DEF 14A / Form 4 / Schedule 13D exists for a China-incorporated dual-listed issuer; the local equivalents used here are the FY2025 Annual Report (SSE, China ASBE, "股东和实际控制人情况" shareholder table) and FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX, IFRS, "董事及行政總裁於股份及相關股份之權益及淡倉" directors'-interests table), both read in their original Chinese and translated, plus CapIQ ownership/insider-activity exports — a non-English filing is disclosed, not opaque (CLAUDE.md §27).

## 1. Ownership

| Holder | % Held | Share Class | Bought or Granted? | Source |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Li Huagang (李华刚) — Chairman, President & CEO | 0.0123% (A: 1,156,699 sh) + 0.0112% (H: 1,049,745 sh) ≈ 0.024% combined | A + H | Granted — A-share increase of 106,255 shares during FY2025 is explicitly coded "员工持股计划归属" (Employee Share Ownership Plan vesting), not an open-market purchase | [FY2025 Annual Report (SSE, CAS), "现任及报告期内离任董事和高级管理人员持股变动及薪酬情况" table, p.64; FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX, IFRS), Directors' Report, p.121-122] |
| Other named directors/officers (aggregate, A-share table only) | 6,159,663 A shares = 0.066% of total share capital, up from 5,496,612 at year-start (+663,051 during FY2025) | A (H-share interests for a subset — Nolan, Yu Hon To, Li Shaohua, Sun Danfeng — disclosed separately, each <0.04% individually) | Granted — the FY2025 increase is attributed line-by-line to "员工持股计划归属" (ESOP vesting) for every director/officer whose holding grew; no line attributes growth to an open-market buy | [FY2025 Annual Report (SSE, CAS), director/officer shareholding table, p.64-65, total row; FY2025 Annual Report (HKEX, IFRS), Directors' Report "於本公司股份的好倉" table, p.121-122] |
| CIQ "Individuals/Insiders" bucket (broader, unnamed beyond directors/officers) | 0.11% (10,042,090 sh) | Mixed A/H (CapIQ aggregate, position dates through 2026) | Not broken out by bought/granted at this aggregation level | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Public Ownership Summary.rtf, Ownership Summary table] |
| Employee Share Ownership Plan (ESOP) vehicle | 1.29% (119,036,057 sh) | A (held via the plan's holding vehicle, distinct from vested shares already sitting in individual directors'/officers' own names above) | Granted (by construction — an ESOP funded via the company's own CNY6,000mn buyback announced 2026-03-26, "为实施员工持股计划" / "to implement the employee share ownership plan," at CNY35/share) | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Public Ownership Summary.rtf, Ownership Summary table; Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Key Developments.xls — Buyback Transaction Announcement, 2026-03-26] |
| Largest institutional (non-controlling) holders | Pzena Investment Management 2.11% (195.2mn sh, position Jun-12-2026); BlackRock, Inc. 2.09% (193.7mn sh, position Jul-31-2026); E Fund Management 1.83% (169.5mn sh, position Jun-30-2025) | A/H (Hong Kong-cleared / mainland Stock Connect and QFII-style holdings) | N/A — passive/active institutional accumulation, not "insider" in the governance sense | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Public Ownership Summary.rtf, Top Holders table] |
| Controlling/promoter-equivalent bloc — Haier Group Corporation + concert parties (see Section 4 for full breakdown) | 34.49% (aggregate direct + concert-party voting rights, per the filing) | A (2,331.3mn sh Haier Group + Haier Kaos) + H (538.6mn sh via HCH (HK) Investment Management) + A (306.0mn sh via two mainland investment vehicles) + a further 0.62% (Haier International Co., Limited) | Founding/inherited ownership from Haier Group's original restructuring into the listed vehicle — not open-market cash purchases, and not RSU-style grants either; this is original controlling-shareholder capital, carried since before the company's listing | [FY2025 Annual Report (SSE, CAS), "股东和实际控制人情况" top-10 shareholder table, p.103-104, and 关联方关系 table, p.228 (企业名称/持股比例/表决权比例)] |

**Total insider/control-group ownership: 34.49%** (Haier Group Corporation + its four disclosed concert-party vehicles, per the FY2025 SSE annual report's own shareholder and related-party tables — this reconciles exactly: 11.44% + 13.42% + 5.74% + 1.84% + 1.43% + 0.62% = 34.49%). This is the filing's own figure and is preferred over the vendor read per source hierarchy (CLAUDE.md §4/§5).

**Reconciliation note on the CapIQ figure.** CapIQ's Public Ownership Summary reports a single "Haier Group Corporation" line of 37.45% (3,466,550,782 sh, position date 2026-03-27) — about 2.96 percentage points (≈233mn shares) above the filing's 34.49% aggregate. Investigating the CapIQ "Top Buyers" table (below) shows the entire apparent +538,560,000-share "increase" attributed to "Haier Group Corporation" matches, share-for-share, the existing stake of HCH (HK) Investment Management Co., Limited (5.74%, a disclosed Haier Group concert party) as shown in the FY2025 annual report's own shareholder table. This is very likely a CapIQ reclassification of an existing concert-party holding into the aggregated "Haier Group Corporation" beneficial-ownership line, not fresh open-market accumulation by the parent — see Section 2 for the full read. The residual ~2.96pp gap between CapIQ's 37.45% and the filing's 34.49% is not explained by any single line item found in the pool and is flagged as an unresolved reconciliation item, not a confirmed additional stake. The filing figure (34.49%) is used as the authoritative total insider/control-group ownership in this report.

**Individual insider ownership is negligible.** Outside the controlling Haier Group bloc, named directors and officers collectively hold 0.066% of total share capital (A-share table) — and every disclosed increase in FY2025 is attributed to Employee Share Ownership Plan vesting, not to a cash purchase. There is no evidence in the pool of any director or officer buying stock in the open market with personal cash.

## 2. Insider Transactions (last 12 months)

| Insider | Buy / Sell | Amount | Date | Notes (10b5-1?) | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|
| "Individuals/Insiders" bucket (CapIQ aggregate, unnamed) | Buys | 2 transactions, 375,748 shares | 2026-05-12 to 2026-08-12 | Not attributable to a named individual in the pool; no China/HK equivalent of a Rule 10b5-1 pre-set trading plan is disclosed either way | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Public Ownership Summary.rtf, "Ownership Activity" / Individuals/Insiders 12-week window] |
| "Individuals/Insiders" bucket (CapIQ aggregate, unnamed) | Sells | 3 transactions, 6,738,647 shares | 2026-05-12 to 2026-08-12 | Not attributable to a named individual in the pool | [same source] |
| "Haier Group Corporation" (CapIQ aggregate line) | Apparent buy | +538,560,000 shares | Position-date change: Dec-31-2025 to Mar-27-2026 | **Not a confirmed open-market purchase.** The exact share count (538,560,000) matches the existing, previously-disclosed stake of HCH (HK) Investment Management Co., Limited — a Haier Group concert party already counted in the annual report's 34.49% aggregate before this "change" date. This reads as a CapIQ reclassification/consolidation of an existing concert-party holding into a single "Haier Group Corporation" line, not new accumulation. Reported here for transparency, but not treated as a genuine conviction-buying signal | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Public Ownership Summary.rtf, "Top Buyers and Sellers" table; cross-checked against FY2025 Annual Report (SSE, CAS), p.103-104, HCH (HK) Investment Management Co., Limited line: 538,560,000 sh, 5.74%] |
| China Securities Finance Corp (Asset Management Arm) | Sell | -54,700,376 shares (change) | trailing period through Mar-31-2026 | Institutional (state financial-stabilization vehicle), not a corporate insider | [Public Ownership Summary.rtf, Top Buyers and Sellers] |
| BlackRock, Inc. | Sell | -40,820,297 shares (change) | trailing period through Jul-31-2026 | Institutional asset manager, not a corporate insider | [same source] |
| Pzena Investment Management, Inc. | Buy | +51,161,554 shares (change) | trailing period through Jun-12-2026 | Institutional value manager, not a corporate insider | [same source] |

**Net insider activity (named individuals / "Individuals/Insiders" bucket): net selling.** Over the trailing ~13 weeks (2026-05-12 to 2026-08-12), 6,738,647 shares were sold against 375,748 shares bought within the CapIQ "Individuals/Insiders" classification — net sales of ~6.36mn shares, about 0.07% of total share capital. No individual names, prices, or stated rationale (option exercise vs. cash sale) are available in the pool at this granularity, so this reads as a low-materiality, low-confidence signal, not a red flag: per this module's evidence-gating rule, a red flag requires a pool filing that names the transaction, and this CapIQ aggregate does not identify the seller(s). The apparent 538.56mn-share "buy" attributed to Haier Group Corporation is, on the evidence above, very likely a reclassification artifact rather than genuine conviction buying, and is not counted as a buying signal.

**No pledging disclosed.** The FY2025 SSE annual report's mandatory disclosure trigger — "controlling shareholder or largest shareholder and its concert parties' cumulative pledged shares ≥80% of their holding" — is explicitly marked "不适用" (Not Applicable) [FY2025 Annual Report (SSE, CAS), "股东和实际控制人情况", p.107], and the top-10 shareholder table's pledge/freeze column reads "无" (none) for every disclosed Haier-affiliated holder. `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` independently computed the pledge ratio at 0% and found no near-miss (well under the ≥40%–≤50% near-miss band). This module does not re-adjudicate that hard disqualifier check (Disqualifier Deference) but confirms the same underlying evidence: pledging is not a governance risk here.

| Feature | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Dual-class / super-voting | No — "Dual Class Stock: No"; domestic (A) shares carry 1 vote each; D- and H-share voting rights are recorded "N/A" in the same field (a currency/listing-venue split, not a super-voting structure) | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Takeover Defenses.xls, "Takeover Defenses" tab] |
| Controlled-company status | Yes, in substance — Haier Group Corporation plus four disclosed concert-party vehicles hold 34.49% aggregate voting rights, well above any single other holder (the next-largest, HKSCC Nominees Limited at 24.66%, is a custodial/nominee account for H-share holders in aggregate, not a single beneficial owner) [FY2025 Annual Report (SSE, CAS), top-10 shareholder table, p.103] | as cited |
| Shareholder bloc with board rights | Haier Group's concert-party bloc controls board nominations in practice: Chairman/CEO Li Huagang, Vice Chairman Gong Wei, and directors Li Shaohua and Kevin Nolan (GE Appliances CEO, a Haier Group operating subsidiary) all hold Haier Group-affiliated executive roles concurrently with their Haier Smart Home board seats [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Board Members.rtf, individual bios] | as cited |
| Bylaw-amendment / M&A approval thresholds | 66.67% shareholder-approval supermajority required to amend bylaws or approve mergers without board approval; any two-or-more shareholders holding ≥10% in aggregate may requisition an EGM; cumulative voting for board seats is permitted (shareholder-protective) | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Takeover Defenses.xls, "Corporate Governance" tab] |
| Poison pill / golden parachutes | None reported; no executive severance or change-of-control agreements with the CEO | [same source] |
| Controlling-owner objective alignment *(Filter 6)* | Haier Group Corporation is registered as a 集体所有制企业 (collectively-owned enterprise, per a 2002 Qingdao State-owned Assets Management Office determination) — not a family promoter, not a central-government SOE with directed-lending or social-policy mandates, and not a sprawling unrelated-diversified conglomerate. Haier Smart Home is the group's primary global listed appliance vehicle (it privatized and absorbed the group's other H-listed subsidiary, Haier Electronics Group, in 2020, rather than being spun off to feed value elsewhere), so it does not fit the "listed subsidiary of a value-maximizing parent" pattern either. On the evidence available, Filter 6 does NOT trip and RF-OWN-004 is not fired. The one genuine alignment watch item — the Group's related-party cash concentration and rising borrowing through the affiliated Haier Group Finance Co. (up to RMB33,988mn parked, RMB3,767mn borrowed against a RMB10,000mn cap) — is a related-party/capital-allocation signal owned by `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`, not an unaligned-owner structural cap; it is noted here for completeness but not double-counted into this module's control-structure score | [FY2025 Annual Report (SSE, CAS), "股东和实际控制人情况" and 关联方 notes, p.107, p.4633-4634; `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`, Section 1, Related-party row] |

**Unaligned-owner test (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 6).** Applying the three structural-conflict patterns: (a) government control — Haier Group is a collectively-owned enterprise, not a central or provincial SOE pursuing directed-lending or social-policy objectives, so this does not apply; (b) listed subsidiary of a value-maximizing parent — Haier Smart Home IS the group's flagship listed operating vehicle (it absorbed the group's other listed appliance entity in 2020 rather than the reverse), so this does not apply; (c) sprawling unrelated-diversified conglomerate — the corporate structure tree shows 136 subsidiary/operating units concentrated in Consumer Discretionary (22) and Industrials (12), consistent with a focused global appliance/smart-home business rather than unrelated diversification [Haier Smart Home Co., Ltd. (SHSE_600690) Corporate Structure Tree.xls, Aggregates tab]. None of the three patterns is triggered on current evidence. This is a company-specific read for this module; it does not override or restate the hard-disqualifier verdict, which belongs to `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` (no disqualifier triggered there either).

| Unusual price / volume before announcements | Not assessed — no daily price/volume series is in this module's pool; this is outside this agent's scope and not cross-checked here | N/A |
| Exchange clarification sought for a price move | None found — the News log and Key Developments log return no matches for exchange-clarification or unusual-movement inquiries | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 News.rtf; Key Developments.xls] |
| Institutional (FII/DII/MF-equivalent) holding trend; any sharp high-quality-holder exit | Mixed, not a sharp exit: institutional ownership is 28.34% of total shares (335 holders, market value CNY 57,055.6mn); Q1-2026 institutional activity shows 52 holders increasing vs 39 decreasing (net new positions +192.6mn sh, decreases -186.5mn sh) — a broadly balanced picture. Individual notable moves: BlackRock reduced by -40,820,297 sh (through Jul-31-2026) and China Securities Finance Corp's asset-management arm reduced by -54,700,376 sh (through Mar-31-2026, a state financial-stabilization fund unwinding a market-support-era position, not necessarily a quality signal), while Pzena Investment Management added +51,161,554 sh (through Jun-12-2026) and the National Social Security Fund ("全国社保基金一一三组合") holds 73,000,090 sh (0.78%) with no reduction flagged | [Public Ownership Summary.rtf, Ownership Activity and Top Buyers/Sellers tables; FY2025 Annual Report (SSE, CAS), top-10 shareholder table, p.103] |

No governance-relevant market-conduct red flag is fired: the individual-insider net selling is small and unattributed, there is no evidence of a sale ahead of a weak result, and institutional flows are mixed rather than a coordinated high-quality-holder exit.

## 5. Ownership / Insider Read

Skin in the game sits almost entirely at the corporate-controller level, not the individual-executive level: Haier Group Corporation and its four disclosed concert-party vehicles hold 34.49% of the company (per the filing's own reconciled figure — the CapIQ-reported 37.45% is a vendor aggregate that does not fully reconcile and is treated as unconfirmed context), while every named director and officer combined holds just 0.066% of shares, virtually all of it received through Employee Share Ownership Plan vesting rather than bought with personal cash. The insider-transaction signal is a mild net sale (about 0.07% of shares outstanding, unnamed, low confidence) once the apparent 538.56mn-share "Haier Group buy" is corrected for what looks like a CapIQ reclassification of an existing concert-party stake rather than genuine new accumulation — so there is no real evidence of insider buying conviction here, and no evidence of urgent insider selling either. The biggest control-related risk to minority holders is not pledging (0%, cleanly disclosed) or a disqualifying unaligned-owner structure (Filter 6 does not trip on current evidence) — it is that the controlling bloc's economic interests are increasingly channeled through affiliated entities (the Haier Group Finance Co. cash/lending relationship already flagged at the business-model level) rather than through direct, cash-funded personal share ownership that would tie individual executives' wealth to the stock price the way a founder-buyer's stake would.

# Board & Shareholder Rights — HAIER

Company: Haier Smart Home Co., Ltd. — dual-primary listed A-share (SSE 600690) and H-share (HKEX 6690), plus a Frankfurt D-share and a US Level-I ADR. No proxy/DEF 14A or SEBI-style AGM notice exists for this China-incorporated dual-listed issuer; the local equivalents used here are the FY2025 Annual Report Board's Report / Corporate Governance section (SSE, China ASBE) and Directors' Report / Connected-Transactions section (HKEX, IFRS) — both read in their original Chinese and translated — plus CapIQ board/committee/ownership/takeover-defenses exports, per CLAUDE.md §27. `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior` has not been produced in this run (only `00`, `01`, and `06` exist alongside this file at the time of writing), so the RF-OWN-004 cross-check below rests on this agent's own read of the ownership data plus `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` and `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`, not on a completed sibling report.

## 1. Board Composition

| Feature | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Board size | 11 directors (excludes the separate PRC Supervisory Board, on which Ma Yingjie sits — a distinct statutory body under Chinese company law, not a board seat) | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Board Members.rtf, current roster] |
| Independent directors (%) | 5 of 11 = **45.5%** independent — Chien Da-Chun, Li Shipeng, Siu Yu Hay, Wu Qi, Wang Hua carry an explicit "Independent Non-Executive Director" / "Independent Director" label. Li Huagang (Chair/CEO), Gong Wei (Vice Chairman), Kevin Nolan (explicitly labeled "Non-Independent Director"), Sun Danfeng (VP/CDO) and Li Shaohua ("Director," no independence label, no bio beyond appointment date) are non-independent executives/insiders. Yu Hon To carries "Non-Executive Director" (no independence label) — see §2 on his interlock with the parent | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Board Members.rtf, per-director Title field] |
| Chair / CEO split? | **No.** Li Huagang holds Chairman of the Board, President, and CEO simultaneously — full power concentration in one role, no separation | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Board Members.rtf, Li, Huagang entry — "Chairman of the Board, President & CEO"] |
| Audit / Comp / Nominating committee independence | Audit: 3 of 5 independent (60%) — includes non-independent Li Shaohua and non-independent Yu Hon To; chaired by independent CPA Siu Yu Hay. Compensation: 3 of 3 independent (100%). Nominating: 3 of 4 independent (75%) — includes executive Sun Danfeng (VP & Chief Digital Officer), an insider sitting on the committee that recommends director candidates | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Committees.rtf, Audit/Compensation/Nominating Committee rosters] |

China's CSRC listing rules require only a minimum one-third independent board — HAIER's 45.5% clears that floor but sits well short of the majority-independent norm used in the US/UK/HK best-practice benchmark. The combined Chair/President/CEO role is common among PRC-listed issuers but is, on its own terms, a real concentration-of-power feature: one person chairs board meetings, sets the agenda, and runs the company.

A fifth new independent director, Siu Yu Hay (Deloitte veteran, Hong Kong CPA), joined Jun-24-2026 replacing Wong Hak Kun on routine six-year term expiry, with committee membership reshuffled the next day | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Board Members.rtf; Key Developments.xls, entries 2026-06-24/25] |

The refreshment is real (5 of 11 current directors joined within the last ~14 months) but is a scheduled multi-year board-election cycle rather than an activist- or crisis-driven shake-up. One thin-disclosure gap: Li Shaohua ("Director," appointed May-2025) carries no independence label and no biographical detail beyond his appointment date in this pool — the same gap this module's `01_management-and-track-record.md` flagged for new CFO Sun Jiacheng. It is a disclosure-depth issue, not by itself an adverse signal, but it means one non-independent audit-committee member cannot be evaluated for conflicts from the data available here.

Materiality read (MODULE_RULES thresholds): the goods/services connected-transaction ratio (5.0% of revenue) sits right at the Medium/High boundary (1–5% Medium, 5–10% High) and is recurring and promoter-linked, so this report treats it as **High** materiality, not merely disclosed-and-fine. The cash-management and borrowing relationship with Haier Group Finance Co. is the more consequential item: parking 57% of Group cash with an affiliated, non-independent finance company concentrates counterparty and liquidity risk inside the controlling shareholder's own financial arm, and the loan balance grew roughly 19x year-on-year (RMB196m → RMB3,767m). None of this clears the `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` 25%-of-revenue/expenses hard-disqualifier threshold (that scan computed 1.18% of revenue on the sales side and 9.55–10.83% of total expenses on a broader purchases reading that also includes the non-consolidated JV Controladora Mabe, which is not promoter-linked) — so this is a **red flag at High severity, not a disqualifier**. It is the single largest self-dealing/leakage channel available to Haier Group and is the one item `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` already identified as "the one signal that would change this classification if it deteriorated."

**RF-RPT-002** (promoter-linked RPT above threshold) and **RF-RPT-003** (loans/advances to related parties) are both triggered — see the Universal Findings Table below.

| Classified / staggered board | Not disclosed as classified | Field reported blank ("-"); 3-year board terms, elected together at the 12th board session (May-2025) with mid-term additions since — no formal staggering mechanism identified | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Takeover Defenses.xls, Corporate Governance tab — "Classified Board: -", "Board Member Term Length (Years): 3"] |
| Dual-class / unequal voting | No | "Dual Class Stock: No"; Domestic (A) shares carry voting-rights weight of 1; D-shares and H-shares fields marked N/A in this export, consistent with one-share-one-vote across share classes for a dual-primary-listed issuer, not a dual-class structure | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Takeover Defenses.xls, Takeover Defenses tab — "Domestic Shares Voting Rights: 1"] |
| Majority vs plurality director voting | Cumulative voting for board seats | "Cumulative Voting for Board Seats: Yes" — a shareholder-protective mechanism that lets a minority bloc concentrate votes on fewer director candidates | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Takeover Defenses.xls, Corporate Governance tab] |
| Shareholder ability to call meetings / act by written consent | Yes, at a 10% threshold | Shareholders holding ≥10% of voting shares (individually or in aggregate) can requisition an extraordinary general meeting; no advance-notice bylaw restricts director nominations; written consent without a meeting is not disclosed as available (field blank) | [Haier Smart Home Co Ltd SHSE 600690 Takeover Defenses.xls, Corporate Governance tab — "% Shareholder Approval Required to Call Special Meetings: 10%"; "Advanced Notice for Director Nominations: No"] |

Other relevant items: no blank-check preferred stock, no executive severance agreements, no change-of-control agreement with the CEO, no golden parachutes — a clean profile with none of the entrenchment devices (poison pill, staggered board, supermajority-protected management, golden parachutes) that typically insulate management from a takeover or a shareholder vote. Bylaw amendments and major mergers require a 66.67% supermajority shareholder vote, which is standard PRC Company Law, not a company-specific defense; combined with Haier Group's ~34.5–37.5% direct-plus-indirect stake (see §1 of `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`), the controlling shareholder alone cannot force through a special resolution without support from part of the free float, but it can single-handedly block one — a standard feature of a controlled-company structure, not an added entrenchment layer.

The dilution picture itself is mild: net share count has drifted down, not up, over the last 15 months, and the employee-share-plan buyback has a built-in cancellation backstop rather than an open-ended dilution mechanism.

## 5. Minority-Shareholder Protection Read

The anti-takeover and voting-mechanics profile is genuinely shareholder-friendly — no poison pill, no dual-class stock, no golden parachutes, cumulative voting for board seats, and a 10%-threshold right to call a special meeting — but this profile mainly protects against a different risk (defensive entrenchment against an outside bidder) than the risk that actually applies here, which is self-dealing by an already-controlling shareholder. On that axis the picture is weaker: the board clears China's one-third independence minimum (45.5%) but falls short of a majority-independent norm, the Chair/President/CEO roles sit in one person with no separation, the Audit Committee is only 60% independent (two non-independent members, one with almost no public biographical disclosure), and the Nominating Committee includes a sitting executive. The single biggest risk to minority holders is the deepening financial relationship with Haier Group Finance Co. — 57% of Group cash parked with an affiliated, non-independent finance company and related-party borrowings up roughly 19x year-on-year (RMB196m to RMB3,767m against a RMB10,000m cap) — a fully disclosed and independent-director-reviewed arrangement today, but one that concentrates counterparty and liquidity risk inside the controlling shareholder's own financial arm rather than diversified independent banks. No hard disqualifier is triggered (`business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` found nothing) and `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior` has not yet produced a finding on RF-OWN-004 in this run — this agent's own read of the ownership data finds no structurally unaligned owner (Haier Group Corporation is a collectively-owned enterprise, not a central-government SOE, not a listed subsidiary of an unrelated value-maximizing parent, and not a sprawling unrelated conglomerate), so no RF-OWN-004 floor applies here. Verdict: **adequate**, weighted down from "strong protection" specifically by the combined Chair/CEO role, the sub-majority-independent audit committee, and the growing related-party cash/lending concentration — none of which is disqualifying today, but any further increase in the Haier Group Finance Co. cap utilization or a weakening of the arm's-length pricing claim would be the trigger that moves this read to "weak / entrenched."

## management-governance / 06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md

_Source: `06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md`_

# Candor & Disclosure Quality — HAIER

**Partial-data note (read this before the sections below):** the data pool contains no verbatim earnings-call transcript newer than Q3 2019 (~82 months stale) and no prior shareholder letter [`00_governance-data-triage.md`, §5; `earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md`, §0]. Per the partial-data rule, a sell-side paraphrase or press release would not substitute even if one existed, and none does — the pool has neither. This means **tone in the worst recent period and Q&A-evasiveness cannot be assessed from primary evidence** and are marked "Not assessable / Insufficient Data" below, with the module-level cap applied (max Disclosure Candor Score of 65, per `00_governance-data-triage.md` §5). What CAN be assessed — and is assessed below on filings alone — is how the FY2025 Annual Report's MD&A / Board's Report narrative and the Q1 2026 Quarterly Report frame the misses documented in `01_management-and-track-record.md` and `earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md`, plus non-GAAP adjustment discipline cross-checked against `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md`.

## 1. Owning the Misses

| Miss (from 01 / earnings/04) | How It Was Communicated | Owned or Obscured? | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| FY2025 full-year EPS missed pre-print consensus by −7.1% (CNY 2.10 actual vs. CNY 2.259 estimate); FY2025 revenue missed by −2.0% | FY2025 Annual Report's Business Review names three specific drags in the narrative — the fading China trade-in subsidy, a weak domestic property market, and US tariffs on China-linked appliance imports — rather than using vague language | **Partly owned.** The named drivers are specific and match the actual macro backdrop, which is candor-positive; but because Haier issues no formal consolidated guidance (§2 below), there is technically no guided number for management to be held to missing, which caps how much credit "naming the drags" can earn — there was no explicit forecast to walk back | [`01_management-and-track-record.md`, §5; `earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md`, §6; FY2025 Annual Report, Business Review] |
| FQ4 2025 (CapIQ-derived quarter) profit trough: net profit fell −59% QoQ (CNY 2,180.1mn vs. CNY 5,339.7mn in Q3 2025), EPS surprise −43.9% | **Not independently explained in the filing as one-off vs. seasonal** — the annual report does not isolate what drove the Q4 concentration of the miss | **Obscured by omission, not by spin.** This is a real disclosure gap rather than active misdirection: management did not construct a misleading narrative, but it also did not explain a −59% sequential profit drop, leaving the magnitude unexplained. Flagged, not resolved, in the upstream earnings-quality read | [`earnings/06_earnings-quality.md`, §5; `earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md`, §6] |
| FQ1 2026 miss on both revenue (−4.9%) and EPS (−2.0%) against consensus, driven partly by North America weakness (winter storms, tariffs) | The Q1 2026 Quarterly Report states that **ex-North-America operating profit grew "over 10%"** in the quarter — a specific, favorable-sounding figure — without reconciling it to a segment-level profit and loss statement anywhere in the pool | **Selectively framed.** This is the single clearest candor concern found: a precise, upbeat statistic is offered for the part of the business that performed well, while the consolidated miss (the actual reported result) goes unaccompanied by an equivalent segment-level breakdown of what specifically went wrong in North America. The claim is dated and attributed to management, but it is unverified against any segment P&L in this pool | [`earnings/06_earnings-quality.md`, §5; `earnings/08_earnings-red-flags.md`, entry "unverified management claim"; `2026 First Quarter Report, Apr-27-2026, p.2`] |
| Two prior, dated, checkable promises (Mar-2025 buyback, 2016 GE Appliances integration) | Both **Kept** — not misses; included here only to show the graded record is not one-sided | Not applicable — these were successes, not misses to own | [`01_management-and-track-record.md`, §4] |

## 2. Non-GAAP / Adjustment Aggressiveness

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Recurring "one-off" addbacks | Government subsidies unrelated to core operations are excluded from the company's officially adjusted ("non-recurring items"-stripped) net profit every year, at a stable magnitude: RMB1,093.6mn (FY2023), RMB1,324.2mn (FY2024), RMB1,315.4mn (FY2025) — roughly 5–7% of reported net profit, three consecutive years, labelled "non-recurring" (非经常性损益) despite the recurring pattern | `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md`, §4–5 |
| GAAP-to-adjusted gap (magnitude / trend) | Adjusted net profit is BELOW reported net profit in every year shown — RMB18,603.6mn adjusted vs. RMB19,552.8mn reported for FY2025 (−4.9%), a consistent −4.6% to −5.0% gap for three straight years. Direction is conservative, not inflationary | `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md`, §7 |
| Does adjusted framing flatter reality? | **No, on direction — the company's own adjustment shrinks reported profit, not inflates it**, which is the opposite of the typical earnings-inflation pattern this check exists to catch. But the recurring-subsidy exclusion, while conservative in direction, is still a labelling issue: calling a three-year-stable RMB1.1–1.3bn/year income stream "non-recurring" understates what the company's true recurring earnings power is, in the direction that makes the "core" number look lower and more conservative than the full recurring picture. A separate, small voluntary supplementary disclosure — an "ex-share-based-compensation" net profit of RMB20,652.7mn, RMB489.7mn above the comparable reported figure — does run in the inflationary direction, but it is disclosed transparently as supplementary colour, not substituted for the headline reported or adjusted numbers, and is immaterial in size (2.4% of reported net profit) | `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md`, §4, §7, §9 |

RF-DISC-002 (recurring "one-off" / aggressive non-GAAP add-backs)

## 3. Disclosure Transparency

Disclosure is genuinely two-tiered. On related-party and financing-arrangement detail — the areas with the most direct governance-risk relevance — Haier discloses in granular, quantified, auditor-reviewed detail: the RMB33,988mn parked with the affiliated Haier Group Finance Co., the RMB3,767mn related-party borrowing increase, and roughly RMB25bn of receivable-factoring and supplier-finance arrangements are all itemized in the notes with independent-director review [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`, §1; `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md`, §8, §10]. But on operating KPIs that would let an outsider verify the story — unit volumes, average selling prices by segment, quarterly EBITDA, or a company-wide commodity-cost sensitivity — nothing is disclosed at any point in the filing history, a structural feature of the CAS/HKEX quarterly-filing regime (revenue, net profit, EPS, and CFO only) rather than a deliberate concealment choice [`earnings/06_earnings-quality.md`, §1; `earnings/08_earnings-red-flags.md`, entries on quarterly-EBITDA and unit-volume gaps]. There is no evidence in this pool of disclosure getting LESS transparent over time — the related-party and factoring notes read as consistently detailed year over year, and the FY2025 filing's naming of specific macro drags (trade-in subsidy fade, property weakness, tariffs) is at least as candid as the sparse commodity/FX sensitivity disclosure that has been a standing gap across the whole earnings module.

## 4. Tone in Bad Times

**Not assessable from primary evidence.** No verbatim transcript exists for any period after Q3 2019, so tone in the worst recent quarter (which was FQ4 2025 or FQ1 2026 on the evidence above) cannot be judged, and whether Q&A questions were answered directly or deflected cannot be tested — there is no Q&A to read. The only tone-adjacent evidence available is the written MD&A narrative in the FY2025 Annual Report, which reads as matter-of-fact rather than promotional (it names specific drags without softening language), but written filing narrative is not a substitute for management's live, unscripted answers under analyst questioning, and this module does not treat it as one.

## 5. Candor Read

Management's written disclosure is more candid than promotional on the two axes that can actually be checked from filings: the FY2025 MD&A names real, specific drags (trade-in subsidy fade, property-market weakness, US tariffs) instead of vague "headwinds" language, and the company's own non-GAAP adjustment runs conservative — it strips gains out of reported profit rather than adding costs back in, the opposite of the earnings-inflation pattern this check exists to catch. Set against that: a recurring ~RMB1.1–1.3bn/year government-subsidy stream has been labelled "non-recurring" for three straight years, a −59% sequential profit drop in the CapIQ-derived FQ4 2025 quarter went unexplained, and the Q1 2026 filing highlighted a specific favorable statistic (">10% ex-North-America operating profit growth") for the part of the business doing well without reconciling it to a segment P&L, while the consolidated print missed consensus. The single biggest candor signal is the absence of any verbatim transcript or Q&A record for the entire FY2025/2026 reporting cycle — this is the one gap that would most change this read, because it is precisely where evasiveness or spin would show up, and it cannot be filled from filings alone. No unresolved adverse integrity signal was routed from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` or noted in `01_management-and-track-record` [`01_management-and-track-record.md`, §4A: "no adverse signal was routed"], so there is no compounding integrity overhang here. Verdict: **mixed** — candid on the written record, but with a real, unverified selective-framing instance and a structural gap (no recent Q&A) that keeps this from being scored "candid / high-trust."

RF-DISC-002 (recurring "one-off" / aggressive non-GAAP add-backs)
RF-DISC-001 (commentary contradicting the numbers)
